📋 Bảng kiểm nội dung cho giáo viên

Đây là nội dung Claude (AI) soạn cho app học tiếng Anh, chưa người thật kiểm (920 mục). Mỗi mục chọn ✅ Đúng hoặc ❌ Sai, ghi chú cách sửa nếu cần. Lựa chọn lưu trên máy của bạn. Xong bấm 📋 Chép kết quả rồi gửi lại cho người làm app.

🧱 Ngữ pháp · câu hỏi hiểu nghĩa (120)

Pre-A1 → A2. Câu hỏi về NGHĨA của câu mẫu (không hỏi dạng). Kiểm: đáp án đúng duy nhất, phương án nhiễu sai rõ.

Động từ to be (am/is/are) pre
My parents are teachers.
❓ Có mấy người là giáo viên?
✔ Hai người (bố và mẹ) · ✗ Một người · ✗ Không ai
Động từ to be (am/is/are) pre
I am not hungry.
❓ Người nói có đói không?
✔ Không đói · ✗ Đang đói · ✗ Không rõ
Đại từ nhân xưng và tính từ sở hữu pre
This is Lan. Her bag is red.
❓ Cái túi màu đỏ là của ai?
✔ Của Lan · ✗ Của người nói · ✗ Của một bạn nam
Đại từ nhân xưng và tính từ sở hữu pre
Tom and I like our school.
❓ “our school” là trường của ai?
✔ Của Tom và người nói · ✗ Chỉ của Tom · ✗ Của người khác
Mạo từ a / an pre
I want an apple.
❓ Người nói muốn mấy quả táo?
✔ Một quả · ✗ Nhiều quả · ✗ Không quả nào
Mạo từ a / an pre
She has a cat.
❓ Cô ấy có mấy con mèo?
✔ Một con · ✗ Hai con · ✗ Không có con nào
Danh từ số nhiều pre
There are three boxes on the table.
❓ Có bao nhiêu cái hộp?
✔ Ba cái · ✗ Một cái · ✗ Không rõ
Danh từ số nhiều pre
The children are in the garden.
❓ Trong vườn có một đứa trẻ hay nhiều đứa trẻ?
✔ Nhiều đứa trẻ · ✗ Một đứa trẻ · ✗ Không có đứa trẻ nào
This / that / these / those pre
These shoes are new.
❓ Đôi giày ở gần hay xa người nói?
✔ Ở gần · ✗ Ở xa · ✗ Không rõ
This / that / these / those pre
Look at that bird!
❓ Con chim ở đâu so với người nói?
✔ Ở xa · ✗ Trong tay người nói · ✗ Ngay cạnh người nói
Have got / has got (sở hữu) pre
Nam has got a bike.
❓ Câu này nói gì về Nam?
✔ Nam có một chiếc xe đạp · ✗ Nam vừa đi xe đạp · ✗ Nam muốn mua xe đạp
Have got / has got (sở hữu) pre
We haven't got a car.
❓ Gia đình người nói có xe hơi không?
✔ Không có · ✗ Có một chiếc · ✗ Có hai chiếc
There is / there are pre
There is a park near my house.
❓ Gần nhà người nói có gì?
✔ Một công viên · ✗ Nhiều công viên · ✗ Không có công viên
There is / there are pre
There aren't any shops in my village.
❓ Làng của người nói có cửa hàng không?
✔ Không có · ✗ Có một cửa hàng · ✗ Có nhiều cửa hàng
Hiện tại đơn: câu khẳng định pre
My father works in a bank.
❓ Câu này nói về việc gì?
✔ Công việc thường ngày của bố · ✗ Việc bố đang làm ngay lúc này · ✗ Việc bố đã làm hôm qua
Hiện tại đơn: câu khẳng định pre
The sun rises in the east.
❓ Câu này nói về điều gì?
✔ Một sự thật luôn đúng · ✗ Một việc chỉ xảy ra hôm nay · ✗ Một dự định
Hiện tại đơn: phủ định và câu hỏi pre
Lan doesn't drink coffee.
❓ Lan có uống cà phê không?
✔ Không, Lan không uống · ✗ Có, Lan uống mỗi ngày · ✗ Lan đang uống
Hiện tại đơn: phủ định và câu hỏi pre
Do you live near here?
❓ Đây là loại câu gì?
✔ Câu hỏi có / không về thói quen, sự thật · ✗ Câu kể · ✗ Câu ra lệnh
Câu hỏi với từ để hỏi (Wh-) pre
Where do you live?
❓ Câu này hỏi về gì?
✔ Nơi ở · ✗ Thời gian · ✗ Tên
Câu hỏi với từ để hỏi (Wh-) pre
Why are you sad?
❓ Câu trả lời phù hợp bắt đầu bằng gì?
✔ Because… · ✗ At seven o'clock · ✗ In Hanoi
Can / can't (khả năng, xin phép) pre
My brother can swim, but he can't ride a bike.
❓ Anh trai làm được việc nào?
✔ Bơi · ✗ Đi xe đạp · ✗ Cả hai việc
Can / can't (khả năng, xin phép) pre
Can I open the window?
❓ Người nói đang làm gì?
✔ Xin phép mở cửa sổ · ✗ Nói mình biết mở cửa sổ · ✗ Ra lệnh mở cửa sổ
Câu mệnh lệnh pre
Close the door, please.
❓ Người nói muốn gì?
✔ Muốn người nghe đóng cửa · ✗ Kể rằng cửa đã đóng · ✗ Hỏi cửa có đóng không
Câu mệnh lệnh pre
Don't touch the oven!
❓ Người nói muốn người nghe làm gì?
✔ Không chạm vào lò · ✗ Chạm vào lò · ✗ Mở lò
Giới từ chỉ nơi chốn pre
The ball is under the bed.
❓ Quả bóng ở đâu?
✔ Dưới gầm giường · ✗ Trên giường · ✗ Cạnh giường
Giới từ chỉ nơi chốn pre
The bank is between the school and the park.
❓ Ngân hàng ở đâu?
✔ Ở giữa trường và công viên · ✗ Đối diện trường · ✗ Phía sau công viên
Hiện tại tiếp diễn pre
Mai is cooking dinner now.
❓ Mai nấu ăn khi nào?
✔ Ngay lúc này · ✗ Mỗi ngày · ✗ Hôm qua
Hiện tại tiếp diễn pre
Look! It's raining.
❓ Trời mưa khi nào?
✔ Đang mưa lúc nói · ✗ Mưa mỗi ngày · ✗ Sẽ mưa ngày mai
Like / love / hate + V-ing pre
I hate getting up early.
❓ Người nói cảm thấy thế nào về việc dậy sớm?
✔ Rất ghét · ✗ Rất thích · ✗ Không quan tâm
Like / love / hate + V-ing pre
My sister loves dancing.
❓ Câu này nói gì về chị gái?
✔ Chị rất thích nhảy · ✗ Chị đang nhảy · ✗ Chị không biết nhảy
Trạng từ chỉ tần suất a1
I usually walk to school.
❓ Người nói đi bộ đến trường bao lâu một lần?
✔ Hầu hết các ngày · ✗ Không bao giờ · ✗ Chỉ hôm nay
Trạng từ chỉ tần suất a1
Tom is never late.
❓ Tom có hay đến muộn không?
✔ Không bao giờ · ✗ Thỉnh thoảng · ✗ Luôn luôn
Giới từ chỉ thời gian: in / on / at a1
My birthday is on 5th May.
❓ “on” đi với loại thời gian nào?
✔ Ngày cụ thể · ✗ Giờ · ✗ Tháng hoặc năm
Giới từ chỉ thời gian: in / on / at a1
The shop closes at nine.
❓ Cửa hàng đóng cửa khi nào?
✔ Lúc 9 giờ · ✗ Vào tháng 9 · ✗ Vào thứ Chín
Sở hữu cách ('s) a1
This is my sister's room.
❓ Căn phòng là của ai?
✔ Của chị / em gái người nói · ✗ Của người nói · ✗ Của nhiều chị em
Sở hữu cách ('s) a1
The students' books are on the desk.
❓ Sách là của một hay nhiều học sinh?
✔ Nhiều học sinh · ✗ Một học sinh · ✗ Của thầy giáo
Đại từ tân ngữ a1
Lan called me yesterday.
❓ Ai gọi điện cho ai?
✔ Lan gọi cho người nói · ✗ Người nói gọi cho Lan · ✗ Lan gọi cho người khác
Đại từ tân ngữ a1
I like them.
❓ “them” chỉ gì?
✔ Nhiều người hoặc vật đã nhắc tới · ✗ Người nói · ✗ Một người đàn ông
Danh từ đếm được, không đếm được; some / any a1
Is there any milk in the fridge?
❓ Người nói đang làm gì?
✔ Hỏi trong tủ lạnh còn sữa không · ✗ Nói tủ lạnh có nhiều sữa · ✗ Nói tủ lạnh hết sữa
Danh từ đếm được, không đếm được; some / any a1
I bought some bread.
❓ Người nói mua bánh mì thế nào?
✔ Một ít, không nói rõ bao nhiêu · ✗ Không mua chút nào · ✗ Đúng một ổ
How much / How many a1
How much does it cost?
❓ Câu này hỏi về gì?
✔ Giá tiền · ✗ Số lượng người · ✗ Thời gian
How much / How many a1
How many students are in your class?
❓ Câu trả lời phù hợp là gì?
✔ Thirty · ✗ Very much · ✗ At eight
Quá khứ của to be: was / were a1
We were at the beach last Sunday.
❓ Việc ở bãi biển xảy ra khi nào?
✔ Trong quá khứ · ✗ Bây giờ · ✗ Sắp tới
Quá khứ của to be: was / were a1
Was the film good?
❓ Người nghe đã xem phim chưa?
✔ Người nghe đã xem xong rồi · ✗ Người nghe sắp xem · ✗ Người nghe đang xem
Quá khứ đơn: câu khẳng định a1
I visited my grandparents last weekend.
❓ Chuyến thăm còn đang diễn ra không?
✔ Không, đã xong trong quá khứ · ✗ Có, vẫn đang thăm · ✗ Sắp diễn ra
Quá khứ đơn: câu khẳng định a1
She lived in Da Nang for two years.
❓ Bây giờ cô ấy có thể vẫn sống ở Đà Nẵng không?
✔ Câu cho thấy việc đó đã kết thúc · ✗ Chắc chắn vẫn sống ở đó · ✗ Cô ấy sắp chuyển tới
Quá khứ đơn: phủ định và câu hỏi a1
Did you see Nam yesterday?
❓ Câu hỏi về thời điểm nào?
✔ Hôm qua (quá khứ) · ✗ Hôm nay · ✗ Ngày mai
Quá khứ đơn: phủ định và câu hỏi a1
We didn't go out last night.
❓ Tối qua họ có đi chơi không?
✔ Không · ✗ Có · ✗ Không rõ
Be going to (dự định, dự đoán có căn cứ) a1
I'm going to visit Japan next year.
❓ Câu này nói về điều gì?
✔ Dự định đã có từ trước · ✗ Việc đã làm năm ngoái · ✗ Việc làm hằng năm
Be going to (dự định, dự đoán có căn cứ) a1
Look at those clouds. It's going to rain.
❓ Vì sao người nói đoán trời sẽ mưa?
✔ Vì nhìn thấy mây (có căn cứ) · ✗ Vì đọc dự báo năm ngoái · ✗ Vì trời đang mưa
Would like (muốn – lịch sự) a1
I'd like a cup of tea, please.
❓ Người nói đang làm gì?
✔ Gọi đồ uống một cách lịch sự · ✗ Nói mình thích trà nói chung · ✗ Từ chối uống trà
Would like (muốn – lịch sự) a1
Would you like to come to my party?
❓ Đây là gì?
✔ Một lời mời · ✗ Một lời xin lỗi · ✗ Một lời ra lệnh
Liên từ: and / but / or / because a1
I was tired, so I went to bed early.
❓ Vì sao người nói đi ngủ sớm?
✔ Vì mệt · ✗ Vì đói · ✗ Không rõ lý do
Liên từ: and / but / or / because a1
You can have tea or coffee.
❓ Người nghe được uống gì?
✔ Một trong hai thứ · ✗ Cả hai thứ · ✗ Không được uống gì
Tính từ và very / really a1
The test was really difficult.
❓ Bài kiểm tra khó tới mức nào?
✔ Rất khó · ✗ Hơi dễ · ✗ Bình thường
Tính từ và very / really a1
It's a very old house.
❓ Căn nhà thế nào?
✔ Rất cũ · ✗ Rất mới · ✗ Không cũ lắm
So sánh hơn của tính từ a1
My sister is older than me.
❓ Ai lớn tuổi hơn?
✔ Chị / em gái · ✗ Người nói · ✗ Bằng tuổi nhau
So sánh hơn của tính từ a1
The bus is cheaper than the train.
❓ Phương tiện nào rẻ hơn?
✔ Xe buýt · ✗ Tàu hoả · ✗ Giá như nhau
So sánh nhất của tính từ a1
Hoa is the tallest girl in our class.
❓ So với cả lớp, Hoa thế nào?
✔ Cao nhất lớp · ✗ Thấp nhất lớp · ✗ Cao bằng các bạn
So sánh nhất của tính từ a1
This is the worst film I've ever seen.
❓ Người nói nghĩ gì về bộ phim?
✔ Dở nhất từng xem · ✗ Hay nhất từng xem · ✗ Khá hay
Have to / don't have to a1
You don't have to bring food.
❓ Có cần mang đồ ăn không?
✔ Không bắt buộc · ✗ Bắt buộc phải mang · ✗ Bị cấm mang
Have to / don't have to a1
I have to wear a uniform at school.
❓ Mặc đồng phục là gì đối với người nói?
✔ Việc bắt buộc · ✗ Việc người nói thích · ✗ Việc bị cấm
Must / mustn't a1
You mustn't use your phone in the exam.
❓ Dùng điện thoại trong phòng thi thì sao?
✔ Bị cấm · ✗ Không bắt buộc · ✗ Được khuyến khích
Must / mustn't a1
Passengers must show their tickets.
❓ Hành khách phải làm gì?
✔ Bắt buộc đưa vé · ✗ Có thể đưa vé nếu muốn · ✗ Không được đưa vé
So sánh bất quy tắc và so sánh bằng a2
Lan is as tall as her mother.
❓ Ai cao hơn?
✔ Hai người cao bằng nhau · ✗ Lan cao hơn · ✗ Mẹ cao hơn
So sánh bất quy tắc và so sánh bằng a2
My cooking is worse than yours.
❓ Ai nấu ngon hơn?
✔ Người nghe · ✗ Người nói · ✗ Ngon như nhau
Trạng từ chỉ cách thức a2
She speaks English fluently.
❓ “fluently” nói về điều gì?
✔ Cách cô ấy nói · ✗ Tính cách của cô ấy · ✗ Số lần cô ấy nói
Trạng từ chỉ cách thức a2
He drove slowly because of the rain.
❓ Anh ấy lái xe thế nào?
✔ Chậm · ✗ Nhanh · ✗ Cẩn thận nhưng nhanh
Hiện tại đơn và hiện tại tiếp diễn a2
I usually take the bus, but today I'm walking.
❓ Hôm nay người nói đi bằng gì?
✔ Đi bộ · ✗ Xe buýt · ✗ Xe máy
Hiện tại đơn và hiện tại tiếp diễn a2
She works in a bank, but this week she's working from home.
❓ Làm ở nhà là việc thế nào?
✔ Tạm thời, chỉ tuần này · ✗ Thói quen lâu dài · ✗ Việc đã xong
Hiện tại tiếp diễn nói về kế hoạch tương lai a2
I'm meeting Tom at six tomorrow.
❓ Câu này nói về điều gì?
✔ Một cuộc hẹn đã sắp xếp trong tương lai · ✗ Việc đang làm lúc nói · ✗ Một thói quen
Hiện tại tiếp diễn nói về kế hoạch tương lai a2
We're flying to Bangkok on Friday.
❓ Chuyến bay đã được lên kế hoạch chưa?
✔ Đã sắp xếp rồi · ✗ Mới chỉ là ý tưởng · ✗ Đã bay xong
Will: dự đoán, đề nghị, quyết định tức thì a2
It's cold. I'll close the window.
❓ Người nói quyết định đóng cửa sổ khi nào?
✔ Ngay lúc nói · ✗ Từ hôm qua · ✗ Từ tuần trước
Will: dự đoán, đề nghị, quyết định tức thì a2
I think it will be sunny tomorrow.
❓ Đây là gì?
✔ Một dự đoán · ✗ Một kế hoạch đã đặt · ✗ Một sự thật đã xảy ra
Will và be going to a2
I've bought the tickets. We're going to see the concert on Saturday.
❓ Việc đi xem hoà nhạc là gì?
✔ Dự định đã chuẩn bị từ trước · ✗ Quyết định ngay lúc nói · ✗ Một lời hứa
Will và be going to a2
The phone's ringing. I'll answer it.
❓ Người nói quyết định nghe máy khi nào?
✔ Ngay lúc đó · ✗ Từ sáng · ✗ Hôm qua
Quá khứ tiếp diễn a2
At eight o'clock last night, I was watching TV.
❓ Lúc 8 giờ tối qua, việc xem TV thế nào?
✔ Đang diễn ra · ✗ Vừa bắt đầu sau 8 giờ · ✗ Đã xong trước 8 giờ
Quá khứ tiếp diễn a2
They were playing football all afternoon.
❓ Họ chơi bóng trong bao lâu?
✔ Suốt buổi chiều (trong quá khứ) · ✗ Một lúc ngắn · ✗ Mỗi buổi chiều
Quá khứ tiếp diễn và quá khứ đơn a2
I was cooking when the phone rang.
❓ Việc nào xảy ra trước và kéo dài?
✔ Nấu ăn · ✗ Điện thoại reo · ✗ Hai việc cùng lúc bắt đầu
Quá khứ tiếp diễn và quá khứ đơn a2
While she was walking home, she saw an accident.
❓ Cô ấy thấy tai nạn khi nào?
✔ Trong lúc đang đi bộ về · ✗ Sau khi về tới nhà · ✗ Trước khi ra khỏi nhà
Hiện tại hoàn thành: kinh nghiệm (ever, never) a2
I have never eaten sushi.
❓ Người nói đã ăn sushi bao giờ chưa?
✔ Chưa bao giờ · ✗ Ăn hôm qua · ✗ Ăn thường xuyên
Hiện tại hoàn thành: kinh nghiệm (ever, never) a2
Have you ever been to Hue?
❓ Câu hỏi về điều gì?
✔ Trải nghiệm trong đời, không cần thời điểm · ✗ Một chuyến đi tuần trước · ✗ Kế hoạch tương lai
Hiện tại hoàn thành với just / already / yet a2
I've just finished my homework.
❓ Người nói làm xong bài khi nào?
✔ Vừa mới xong · ✗ Từ tuần trước · ✗ Chưa xong
Hiện tại hoàn thành với just / already / yet a2
Have you called her yet?
❓ Người hỏi nghĩ gì?
✔ Có thể người nghe chưa gọi · ✗ Chắc chắn đã gọi từ lâu · ✗ Sẽ không bao giờ gọi
Hiện tại hoàn thành và quá khứ đơn a2
I have lost my keys.
❓ Bây giờ người nói có chìa khoá không?
✔ Không, vẫn đang mất · ✗ Có, đã tìm thấy · ✗ Không rõ
Hiện tại hoàn thành và quá khứ đơn a2
I lost my keys last week, but I found them.
❓ Bây giờ người nói có chìa khoá không?
✔ Có, đã tìm thấy · ✗ Không, vẫn đang mất · ✗ Chưa bao giờ có chìa khoá
Should / shouldn't (lời khuyên) a2
You should see a doctor.
❓ Đây là gì?
✔ Một lời khuyên · ✗ Một mệnh lệnh bắt buộc · ✗ Một lời cấm
Should / shouldn't (lời khuyên) a2
You shouldn't eat so much sugar.
❓ Người nói nghĩ gì?
✔ Ăn nhiều đường là không tốt · ✗ Ăn nhiều đường là bắt buộc · ✗ Ăn đường bị cấm theo luật
Could / can: nhờ, xin phép, khả năng trong quá khứ a2
Could you help me with this bag?
❓ Người nói đang làm gì?
✔ Nhờ giúp một cách lịch sự · ✗ Nói mình từng giúp được · ✗ Ra lệnh
Could / can: nhờ, xin phép, khả năng trong quá khứ a2
When I was five, I could read.
❓ Câu này nói về điều gì?
✔ Khả năng trong quá khứ · ✗ Lời xin phép · ✗ Dự định
Lượng từ: much, many, a lot of, a few, a little a2
There are a few eggs left.
❓ Còn trứng không?
✔ Còn một ít · ✗ Hết sạch · ✗ Còn rất nhiều
Lượng từ: much, many, a lot of, a few, a little a2
I have little time today.
❓ Hôm nay người nói có nhiều thời gian không?
✔ Có rất ít, gần như không đủ · ✗ Có nhiều thời gian · ✗ Có vừa đủ
Too / enough a2
This box is too heavy for me to carry.
❓ Người nói có mang được cái hộp không?
✔ Không mang được · ✗ Mang được dễ dàng · ✗ Đang mang
Too / enough a2
She is old enough to drive.
❓ Cô ấy có được lái xe không?
✔ Đủ tuổi để lái · ✗ Chưa đủ tuổi · ✗ Quá tuổi
Động từ + to V a2
I decided to stay at home.
❓ Người nói đã làm gì?
✔ Quyết định ở nhà · ✗ Ra ngoài chơi · ✗ Chưa quyết định
Động từ + to V a2
We hope to see you soon.
❓ Câu này thể hiện điều gì?
✔ Mong muốn gặp lại · ✗ Một việc đã xảy ra · ✗ Một lời từ chối
Động từ + V-ing a2
He stopped smoking last year.
❓ Bây giờ anh ấy có hút thuốc không?
✔ Không, đã bỏ · ✗ Có, vẫn hút · ✗ Vừa bắt đầu hút
Động từ + V-ing a2
I enjoy swimming in the sea.
❓ Người nói thấy bơi ở biển thế nào?
✔ Thích · ✗ Ghét · ✗ Sợ
Câu điều kiện loại 0 a2
If you mix red and blue, you get purple.
❓ Câu này nói về điều gì?
✔ Một sự thật luôn đúng · ✗ Một khả năng chỉ xảy ra một lần · ✗ Một điều tưởng tượng
Câu điều kiện loại 0 a2
If it rains, the streets get wet.
❓ Khi nào đường bị ướt?
✔ Mỗi khi trời mưa · ✗ Chỉ hôm nay · ✗ Không bao giờ
Câu điều kiện loại 1 a2
If it rains tomorrow, we'll stay at home.
❓ Ngày mai họ có chắc chắn ở nhà không?
✔ Chỉ ở nhà nếu trời mưa · ✗ Chắc chắn ở nhà · ✗ Chắc chắn ra ngoài
Câu điều kiện loại 1 a2
If you don't hurry, you'll miss the bus.
❓ Người nói muốn người nghe làm gì?
✔ Nhanh lên · ✗ Đi chậm lại · ✗ Bỏ chuyến xe
Đại từ bất định: something, anyone, nobody… a2
Nobody called me today.
❓ Hôm nay có ai gọi không?
✔ Không ai gọi · ✗ Một người gọi · ✗ Nhiều người gọi
Đại từ bất định: something, anyone, nobody… a2
I want to eat something sweet.
❓ Người nói muốn ăn gì?
✔ Một món ngọt nào đó, chưa rõ món gì · ✗ Một món ngọt cụ thể · ✗ Không ăn gì
Đại từ sở hữu: mine, yours… a2
Is this pen yours?
❓ Người nói hỏi gì?
✔ Cây bút có phải của người nghe không · ✗ Người nghe có bút không · ✗ Người nghe muốn mua bút không
Đại từ sở hữu: mine, yours… a2
That car isn't ours.
❓ Chiếc xe là của ai?
✔ Không phải của nhóm người nói · ✗ Của nhóm người nói · ✗ Của người nói một mình
Mạo từ the và không dùng mạo từ a2
I saw a dog in the park. The dog was very big.
❓ Vì sao câu hai dùng “the dog”?
✔ Vì con chó đã được nhắc tới · ✗ Vì là con chó duy nhất trên đời · ✗ Vì nói về chó nói chung
Mạo từ the và không dùng mạo từ a2
Dogs are friendly animals.
❓ Câu này nói về chó nào?
✔ Chó nói chung · ✗ Một con chó cụ thể · ✗ Con chó của người nói
Từ nối trình tự: first, then, after that, finally a2
First, wash the rice. Then, put it in the pot.
❓ Việc nào làm trước?
✔ Vo gạo · ✗ Cho gạo vào nồi · ✗ Làm cùng lúc
Từ nối trình tự: first, then, after that, finally a2
After that, we had lunch. Finally, we went home.
❓ Việc nào làm cuối cùng?
✔ Về nhà · ✗ Ăn trưa · ✗ Không rõ
Cụm động từ thông dụng a2
Please turn off the lights.
❓ Người nói muốn gì?
✔ Tắt đèn · ✗ Bật đèn · ✗ Đổi bóng đèn
Cụm động từ thông dụng a2
I gave up chocolate for a month.
❓ Người nói đã làm gì?
✔ Bỏ ăn sô-cô-la một tháng · ✗ Tặng sô-cô-la · ✗ Ăn nhiều sô-cô-la
Giới từ chỉ chuyển động a2
The cat jumped onto the table.
❓ Con mèo chuyển động thế nào?
✔ Nhảy từ dưới lên mặt bàn · ✗ Nằm yên trên bàn · ✗ Nhảy từ bàn xuống
Giới từ chỉ chuyển động a2
She ran out of the house.
❓ Cô ấy đi đâu?
✔ Từ trong nhà ra ngoài · ✗ Từ ngoài vào trong nhà · ✗ Đi quanh nhà
Trật tự tính từ a2
He has a lovely old wooden table.
❓ Từ nào là ý kiến của người nói?
✔ lovely · ✗ old · ✗ wooden
Trật tự tính từ a2
She bought a small black leather bag.
❓ Cái túi làm bằng gì?
✔ Da · ✗ Vải đen · ✗ Nhựa
Mệnh đề quan hệ: who / which / that / where a2
The man who lives next door is a doctor.
❓ Ai là bác sĩ?
✔ Người đàn ông sống bên cạnh · ✗ Người nói · ✗ Ai đó ở xa
Mệnh đề quan hệ: who / which / that / where a2
This is the café where we first met.
❓ Quán cà phê này đặc biệt vì sao?
✔ Là nơi họ gặp nhau lần đầu · ✗ Là quán gần nhà · ✗ Là quán mới mở
Câu bị động: hiện tại đơn và quá khứ đơn a2
The bridge was built in 1990.
❓ Câu này nhấn mạnh điều gì?
✔ Cây cầu và thời điểm xây, không phải ai xây · ✗ Người xây cầu · ✗ Cây cầu đang được xây
Câu bị động: hiện tại đơn và quá khứ đơn a2
English is spoken in many countries.
❓ Ai nói tiếng Anh?
✔ Người ở nhiều nước (không cần nói rõ là ai) · ✗ Chỉ người Anh · ✗ Không ai nói

🔀 Ngữ pháp · nhóm điểm dễ nhầm (22)

Các điểm trong nhóm có thật sự hay bị nhầm với nhau không; nên thêm / bớt điểm nào.

Hiện tại đơn hay hiện tại tiếp diễn
Hiện tại đơn: câu khẳng định · Hiện tại tiếp diễn · Hiện tại đơn và hiện tại tiếp diễn
Quá khứ đơn hay hiện tại hoàn thành
Quá khứ đơn: câu khẳng định · Hiện tại hoàn thành: kinh nghiệm (ever, never) · Hiện tại hoàn thành với just / already / yet · Hiện tại hoàn thành và quá khứ đơn
Quá khứ đơn hay quá khứ tiếp diễn
Quá khứ đơn: câu khẳng định · Quá khứ tiếp diễn · Quá khứ tiếp diễn và quá khứ đơn
Will, be going to hay hiện tại tiếp diễn (tương lai)
Be going to (dự định, dự đoán có căn cứ) · Hiện tại tiếp diễn nói về kế hoạch tương lai · Will: dự đoán, đề nghị, quyết định tức thì · Will và be going to
A / an, the hay không mạo từ
Mạo từ a / an · Mạo từ the và không dùng mạo từ
Some / any, much / many, a few / a little
Danh từ đếm được, không đếm được; some / any · How much / How many · Lượng từ: much, many, a lot of, a few, a little
Have to, must hay should
Have to / don't have to · Must / mustn't · Should / shouldn't (lời khuyên)
Giới từ thời gian, nơi chốn, chuyển động
Giới từ chỉ nơi chốn · Giới từ chỉ thời gian: in / on / at · Giới từ chỉ chuyển động
So sánh hơn, so sánh nhất, so sánh bằng
So sánh hơn của tính từ · So sánh nhất của tính từ · So sánh bất quy tắc và so sánh bằng
To V hay V-ing
Động từ + to V · Động từ + V-ing · Động từ + to V hay V-ing đổi nghĩa
Câu điều kiện loại 0, 1, 2
Câu điều kiện loại 0 · Câu điều kiện loại 1 · Câu điều kiện loại 2
Câu điều kiện loại 2, 3, hỗn hợp
Câu điều kiện loại 2 · Câu điều kiện loại 3 · Câu điều kiện hỗn hợp
Hiện tại hoàn thành hay hiện tại hoàn thành tiếp diễn
Hiện tại hoàn thành: kinh nghiệm (ever, never) · Hiện tại hoàn thành với just / already / yet · Hiện tại hoàn thành tiếp diễn
Quá khứ đơn hay quá khứ hoàn thành
Quá khứ đơn: câu khẳng định · Quá khứ tiếp diễn và quá khứ đơn · Quá khứ hoàn thành
Used to, be used to hay get used to
Used to / would: thói quen trong quá khứ · Be used to / get used to
Suy đoán hiện tại hay quá khứ (must / might / can't)
Suy đoán hiện tại: must / might / can't · Suy đoán quá khứ: must have / might have / can't have
Wish cho hiện tại hay quá khứ
Wish + quá khứ (ước cho hiện tại) · Wish / if only cho quá khứ và would
But, although, despite hay whereas
Liên từ: and / but / or / because · Although / despite / in spite of · Từ nối trang trọng: whereas, albeit, hence, nonetheless
Mệnh đề quan hệ xác định hay không xác định
Mệnh đề quan hệ: who / which / that / where · Mệnh đề quan hệ không xác định
Bị động các dạng và have something done
Câu bị động: hiện tại đơn và quá khứ đơn · Bị động các thì khác · Bị động tường thuật: It is said that / is said to · Have / get something done
Should have, needn't have hay didn't need to
Should have / could have / needn't have · Needn't have / didn't need to
Các kiểu đảo ngữ
Đảo ngữ sau trạng từ phủ định · Đảo ngữ điều kiện (bỏ if) · No sooner … than / Hardly … when · Đảo ngữ: Little did / Only then / So … that

🔗 Viết · nối câu (120)

Câu nối có tự nhiên không; danh sách đáp án được chấp nhận đã đủ chưa (thiếu cách viết đúng nào thì ghi vào ghi chú).

A1 I like apples. I don't like bananas. · dùng but
✔ I like apples, but I don't like bananas.
A1 She is tired. She goes to bed. · dùng so
✔ She is tired, so she goes to bed.
A1 I stay at home. It is raining. · dùng because
✔ I stay at home because it is raining.
✔ Because it is raining, I stay at home.
A1 We can play football. We can watch TV. · dùng or
✔ We can play football or watch TV.
✔ We can play football, or we can watch TV.
A1 My brother is tall. He plays basketball. · dùng and
✔ My brother is tall and he plays basketball.
✔ My brother is tall and plays basketball.
A1 The shop is open. The café is closed. · dùng but
✔ The shop is open, but the café is closed.
A1 I am hungry. I eat a sandwich. · dùng so
✔ I am hungry, so I eat a sandwich.
A1 He is happy. It is his birthday. · dùng because
✔ He is happy because it is his birthday.
✔ Because it is his birthday, he is happy.
A1 Do you want tea? Do you want coffee? · dùng or
✔ Do you want tea or coffee?
✔ Do you want tea or do you want coffee?
A1 I have a cat. I have a dog. · dùng and
✔ I have a cat and a dog.
✔ I have a cat and I have a dog.
A1 The film was long. It was very good. · dùng but
✔ The film was long, but it was very good.
✔ The film was long but very good.
A1 It is cold. I wear a coat. · dùng so
✔ It is cold, so I wear a coat.
A1 She can't come. She is ill. · dùng because
✔ She can't come because she is ill.
✔ Because she is ill, she can't come.
A1 We go to the park. We have a picnic. · dùng and
✔ We go to the park and have a picnic.
✔ We go to the park and we have a picnic.
A1 My room is small. It is very nice. · dùng but
✔ My room is small, but it is very nice.
✔ My room is small but very nice.
A1 The bus was late. I walked to school. · dùng so
✔ The bus was late, so I walked to school.
A1 I like summer. I can swim in the sea. · dùng because
✔ I like summer because I can swim in the sea.
✔ Because I can swim in the sea, I like summer.
A1 You can call me. You can send a message. · dùng or
✔ You can call me or send a message.
✔ You can call me, or you can send a message.
A1 Tom gets up at seven. He has breakfast. · dùng and
✔ Tom gets up at seven and has breakfast.
✔ Tom gets up at seven and he has breakfast.
A1 I want to buy the shoes. They are too expensive. · dùng but
✔ I want to buy the shoes, but they are too expensive.
A2 I was eating dinner. The phone rang. · dùng when
✔ I was eating dinner when the phone rang.
✔ When the phone rang, I was eating dinner.
A2 Wash your hands. You eat. · dùng before
✔ Wash your hands before you eat.
✔ Before you eat, wash your hands.
✔ Wash your hands before eating.
✔ Before eating, wash your hands.
A2 We watched a film. We finished our homework. · dùng after
✔ We watched a film after we finished our homework.
✔ After we finished our homework, we watched a film.
✔ We watched a film after we had finished our homework.
✔ After we had finished our homework, we watched a film.
✔ After finishing our homework, we watched a film.
✔ We watched a film after finishing our homework.
A2 It rains tomorrow. We will stay at home. · dùng if
✔ If it rains tomorrow, we will stay at home.
✔ We will stay at home if it rains tomorrow.
A2 Wait here. I come back. · dùng until
✔ Wait here until I come back.
A2 She listened to music. She was cooking. · dùng while
✔ She listened to music while she was cooking.
✔ While she was cooking, she listened to music.
A2 He was late. He missed the bus. · dùng because
✔ He was late because he missed the bus.
✔ Because he missed the bus, he was late.
A2 I'll call you. I arrive at the station. · dùng when
✔ I'll call you when I arrive at the station.
✔ When I arrive at the station, I'll call you.
A2 You don't hurry. You will miss the train. · dùng if
✔ If you don't hurry, you will miss the train.
✔ You will miss the train if you don't hurry.
A2 The weather was bad. We went for a walk. · dùng but
✔ The weather was bad, but we went for a walk.
A2 I read a book. I go to sleep. · dùng before
✔ I read a book before I go to sleep.
✔ Before I go to sleep, I read a book.
✔ I read a book before going to sleep.
✔ Before going to sleep, I read a book.
A2 He felt better. He took the medicine. · dùng after
✔ He felt better after he took the medicine.
✔ After he took the medicine, he felt better.
✔ He felt better after he had taken the medicine.
✔ After he had taken the medicine, he felt better.
✔ After taking the medicine, he felt better.
✔ He felt better after taking the medicine.
A2 Keep walking. You see the bank. · dùng until
✔ Keep walking until you see the bank.
A2 My dad was sleeping. I was watching TV. · dùng while
✔ My dad was sleeping while I was watching TV.
✔ While I was watching TV, my dad was sleeping.
✔ While my dad was sleeping, I was watching TV.
✔ I was watching TV while my dad was sleeping.
A2 The tickets were cheap. We bought four. · dùng so
✔ The tickets were cheap, so we bought four.
A2 She was a child. She lived in Hue. · dùng when
✔ When she was a child, she lived in Hue.
✔ She lived in Hue when she was a child.
A2 You need help. Ask your teacher. · dùng if
✔ If you need help, ask your teacher.
✔ Ask your teacher if you need help.
A2 I didn't go out. I had a lot of homework. · dùng because
✔ I didn't go out because I had a lot of homework.
✔ Because I had a lot of homework, I didn't go out.
A2 Turn off the lights. You leave the room. · dùng when
✔ Turn off the lights when you leave the room.
✔ When you leave the room, turn off the lights.
A2 We stayed on the beach. The sun went down. · dùng until
✔ We stayed on the beach until the sun went down.
B1 It was raining. We played tennis. · dùng although
✔ Although it was raining, we played tennis.
✔ We played tennis although it was raining.
B1 I have a friend. She speaks four languages. · dùng who
✔ I have a friend who speaks four languages.
B1 This is the book. I told you about it. · dùng which
✔ This is the book which I told you about.
✔ This is the book that I told you about.
✔ This is the book about which I told you.
B1 That's the hotel. We stayed there last year. · dùng where
✔ That's the hotel where we stayed last year.
B1 You won't pass. You study harder. · dùng unless
✔ You won't pass unless you study harder.
✔ Unless you study harder, you won't pass.
B1 Call me. You get home. · dùng as soon as
✔ Call me as soon as you get home.
✔ As soon as you get home, call me.
B1 She saved money. She could buy a laptop. · dùng so that
✔ She saved money so that she could buy a laptop.
B1 He failed the test. He studied a lot. · dùng even though
✔ He failed the test even though he studied a lot.
✔ Even though he studied a lot, he failed the test.
B1 The man is my uncle. He is wearing a red hat. · dùng who
✔ The man who is wearing a red hat is my uncle.
✔ The man that is wearing a red hat is my uncle.
B1 I lost the watch. My grandfather gave it to me. · dùng which
✔ I lost the watch which my grandfather gave to me.
✔ I lost the watch that my grandfather gave to me.
✔ I lost the watch which my grandfather gave me.
✔ I lost the watch that my grandfather gave me.
B1 You can borrow my bike. You bring it back tomorrow. · dùng as long as
✔ You can borrow my bike as long as you bring it back tomorrow.
✔ As long as you bring it back tomorrow, you can borrow my bike.

✔ Although the restaurant was full, we found a table.

🗣 Nói Pre-A1 (23)

Đề có hợp trẻ / người mới (kiểu Starters) không; bài mẫu đúng và tự nhiên không.

My Name and Age · Tên và tuổi
Say your name and how old you are.
Ý: Tên · Tuổi
Mẫu: Hello! My name is Lan. I am eight years old.
My Favourite Colour · Màu yêu thích
Say your favourite colour and one thing with that colour.
Ý: Màu yêu thích · Một đồ vật màu đó
Mẫu: My favourite colour is blue. My bag is blue and my bike is blue too.
My Family · Gia đình tôi
Say who is in your family.
Ý: Bố mẹ · Anh chị em hoặc ông bà
Mẫu: There are four people in my family: my mum, my dad, my sister and me.
My Pet · Con vật nuôi
Say what pet you have or want, and what it looks like.
Ý: Con vật · Màu hoặc kích thước
Mẫu: I have a dog. It is small and brown. Its name is Bo.
Food I Like · Món tôi thích
Say two foods you like and one food you don't like.
Ý: Món thích · Món không thích
Mẫu: I like rice and chicken. I don't like fish.
My Classroom · Lớp học của tôi
Say what is in your classroom.
Ý: Đồ vật trong lớp · Số lượng
Mẫu: In my classroom there is a big board. There are twenty desks and a lot of books.
My House · Nhà của tôi
Say what rooms are in your house and your favourite room.
Ý: Các phòng · Phòng thích nhất
Mẫu: My house has a kitchen, a living room and two bedrooms. My favourite room is my bedroom.
Animals at the Zoo · Con vật ở sở thú
Say two animals you like at the zoo and why.
Ý: Hai con vật · Vì sao thích
Mẫu: I like monkeys and elephants. Monkeys are funny and elephants are very big.
I Can · Tôi biết làm
Say two things you can do and one thing you can't do.
Ý: Việc biết làm · Việc chưa biết làm
Mẫu: I can swim and I can ride a bike. I can't play the piano.
My Friend · Bạn của tôi
Say your friend's name and what you play together.
Ý: Tên bạn · Chơi gì cùng nhau
Mẫu: My friend's name is Nam. We play football after school.
My Clothes Today · Quần áo hôm nay
Say what you are wearing today and the colours.
Ý: Quần áo · Màu
Mẫu: Today I'm wearing a white T-shirt, black trousers and red shoes.
My Birthday · Sinh nhật của tôi
Say when your birthday is and what you like to eat on your birthday.
Ý: Tháng sinh nhật · Món thích ăn
Mẫu: My birthday is in June. On my birthday I like cake and ice cream.
My Toys · Đồ chơi của tôi
Say two toys you have and which one you like best.
Ý: Hai đồ chơi · Thích nhất
Mẫu: I have a ball and a robot. I like my robot best because it can walk.
The Weather Today · Thời tiết hôm nay
Say what the weather is like today and what you can do.
Ý: Thời tiết · Việc có thể làm
Mẫu: It's sunny and hot today. I can go to the park and play.
My School Bag · Cặp sách của tôi
Say what is in your school bag.
Ý: Đồ trong cặp · Màu hoặc số lượng
Mẫu: In my school bag there are three books, a pencil case and a red ruler.
Câu đọc to (thi): This is my red bag.
Câu đọc to (thi): I have two cats.
Câu đọc to (thi): My mum is a teacher.
Câu đọc to (thi): The ball is under the table.
Câu đọc to (thi): I like apples and bananas.
Câu đọc to (thi): We play in the garden.
Câu đọc to (thi): Look at the big elephant!
Câu đọc to (thi): Can you see the bird?

👄 Phát âm B1 → C2 · cặp âm (13)

Phiên âm (kiểu Anh) đúng không; cặp có thật sự chỉ khác âm đang luyện; mẹo đặt miệng đúng không.

jet – yet B1 · /dʒ/ – /j/
jet /dʒet/ – yet /jet/ · jam /dʒæm/ – yam /jæm/ · joke /dʒəʊk/ – yolk /jəʊk/ · jeer /dʒɪə/ – year /jɪə/ · jack /dʒæk/ – yak /jæk/ · jello /ˈdʒeləʊ/ – yellow /ˈjeləʊ/ · jot /dʒɒt/ – yacht /jɒt/
Mẹo: Tiếng Việt “gi / d” (miền Bắc đọc như /z/) dễ lẫn. /dʒ/ chu môi, bật mạnh như “ch” có rung (jet); /j/ lướt nhẹ như “y” trong “yêu” (yet).
boy – buy B1 · /ɔɪ/ – /aɪ/
boy /bɔɪ/ – buy /baɪ/ · toy /tɔɪ/ – tie /taɪ/ · foil /fɔɪl/ – file /faɪl/ · voice /vɔɪs/ – vice /vaɪs/ · oil /ɔɪl/ – aisle /aɪl/ · soy /sɔɪ/ – sigh /saɪ/ · loin /lɔɪn/ – line /laɪn/
Mẹo: /ɔɪ/ bắt đầu “o” tròn môi rồi lướt sang “i” (như “oi” trong “con voi”); /aɪ/ bắt đầu “a” mở miệng rồi lướt sang “i” (như “ai”).
now – know B1 · /aʊ/ – /əʊ/
now /naʊ/ – know /nəʊ/ · town /taʊn/ – tone /təʊn/ · loud /laʊd/ – load /ləʊd/ · found /faʊnd/ – phoned /fəʊnd/ · how /haʊ/ – hoe /həʊ/ · couch /kaʊtʃ/ – coach /kəʊtʃ/ · crowd /kraʊd/ – crowed /krəʊd/
Mẹo: /aʊ/ mở miệng rộng “a” rồi tròn môi về “u” (như “ao”); /əʊ/ bắt đầu “ơ” rồi tròn môi về “u” (gần “âu”, nhẹ hơn).
chain – train B1 · /tʃ/ – /tr/
chain /tʃeɪn/ – train /treɪn/ · chip /tʃɪp/ – trip /trɪp/ · chew /tʃuː/ – true /truː/ · cheese /tʃiːz/ – trees /triːz/ · chick /tʃɪk/ – trick /trɪk/ · chap /tʃæp/ – trap /træp/ · chunk /tʃʌŋk/ – trunk /trʌŋk/
Mẹo: Miền Bắc đọc “tr” giống “ch” nên dễ lẫn. /tʃ/ là MỘT âm: chu môi, bật “ch”; /tr/ là HAI âm: /t/ rồi /r/ cong lưỡi (train), gần “tr” miền Nam.
tray – stray B2 · 2 phụ âm đầu – 3 phụ âm đầu
tray /treɪ/ – stray /streɪ/ · cream /kriːm/ – scream /skriːm/ · train /treɪn/ – strain /streɪn/ · pray /preɪ/ – spray /spreɪ/ · crew /kruː/ – screw /skruː/ · trip /trɪp/ – strip /strɪp/ · trap /træp/ – strap /stræp/
Mẹo: Đừng chèn nguyên âm vào giữa (“sờ-trây”): /s/ xì nhẹ rồi bật luôn /tr/, /kr/, /pr/ trong một hơi.
walk – walked B2 · không đuôi -ed – có đuôi -ed
walk /wɔːk/ – walked /wɔːkt/ · play /pleɪ/ – played /pleɪd/ · want /wɒnt/ – wanted /ˈwɒntɪd/ · stop /stɒp/ – stopped /stɒpt/ · rob /rɒb/ – robbed /rɒbd/ · laugh /lɑːf/ – laughed /lɑːft/ · need /niːd/ – needed /ˈniːdɪd/
Mẹo: -ed đọc /t/ sau âm vô thanh (walked), /d/ sau âm hữu thanh (played), /ɪd/ sau /t/ /d/ (wanted). Người Việt hay bỏ đuôi: đọc rõ rồi nối sang từ sau.
sing – sink B2 · /ŋ/ cuối – /ŋk/ cuối
sing /sɪŋ/ – sink /sɪŋk/ · thing /θɪŋ/ – think /θɪŋk/ · wing /wɪŋ/ – wink /wɪŋk/ · rang /ræŋ/ – rank /ræŋk/ · bang /bæŋ/ – bank /bæŋk/ · sung /sʌŋ/ – sunk /sʌŋk/ · ping /pɪŋ/ – pink /pɪŋk/
Mẹo: /ŋ/ như “ng” tiếng Việt, ngân ở mũi (sing); /ŋk/ thêm /k/ bật nhẹ ở cuối (sink). Đừng nuốt /k/.
safe – save B2 · /f/ cuối – /v/ cuối
safe /seɪf/ – save /seɪv/ · proof /pruːf/ – prove /pruːv/ · belief /bɪˈliːf/ – believe /bɪˈliːv/ · leaf /liːf/ – leave /liːv/ · grief /ɡriːf/ – grieve /ɡriːv/ · calf /kɑːf/ – calve /kɑːv/ · strife /straɪf/ – strive /straɪv/
Mẹo: /v/ cuối vẫn rung (răng trên chạm môi dưới, rung nhẹ, âm trước kéo dài hơn); /f/ chỉ thổi hơi. Người Việt hay đọc /v/ cuối thành /f/ hoặc bỏ.
beer – bear C1 · /ɪə/ – /eə/
beer /bɪə/ – bear /beə/ · here /hɪə/ – hair /heə/ · fear /fɪə/ – fair /feə/ · cheer /tʃɪə/ – chair /tʃeə/ · pier /pɪə/ – pear /peə/ · steer /stɪə/ – stare /steə/ · rear /rɪə/ – rare /reə/
Mẹo: Giọng Anh: /ɪə/ bắt đầu “i” rồi lướt về “ơ” (beer); /eə/ bắt đầu “e” rồi lướt về “ơ” (bear). Giọng Mỹ thêm /r/ cuối.
text – texts C1 · ít phụ âm cuối – nhiều phụ âm cuối
text /tekst/ – texts /teksts/ · ask /ɑːsk/ – asked /ɑːskt/ · month /mʌnθ/ – months /mʌnθs/ · help /help/ – helped /helpt/ · world /wɜːld/ – worlds /wɜːldz/ · fifth /fɪfθ/ – fifths /fɪfθs/ · risk /rɪsk/ – risks /rɪsks/
Mẹo: Đọc đủ từng phụ âm cuối, không thêm nguyên âm: texts = t-e-k-s-t-s. Đọc chậm trước, rồi nhanh dần.
three – free C1 · /θ/ – /f/
three /θriː/ – free /friː/ · thought /θɔːt/ – fought /fɔːt/ · thin /θɪn/ – fin /fɪn/ · death /deθ/ – deaf /def/ · thirst /θɜːst/ – first /fɜːst/ · thrill /θrɪl/ – frill /frɪl/ · oath /əʊθ/ – oaf /əʊf/
Mẹo: /θ/ đặt đầu lưỡi giữa hai răng rồi thổi (three); /f/ răng trên chạm môi dưới (free). Đừng đọc /θ/ thành “ph” hay “th” tiếng Việt.
breeze – breathe C2 · /z/ – /ð/
breeze /briːz/ – breathe /briːð/ · tease /tiːz/ – teethe /tiːð/ · bays /beɪz/ – bathe /beɪð/ · seize /siːz/ – seethe /siːð/ · zen /zen/ – then /ðen/ · sues /suːz/ – soothe /suːð/ · lows /ləʊz/ – loathe /ləʊð/
Mẹo: /ð/ đầu lưỡi giữa hai răng và rung (breathe, then); /z/ lưỡi sau răng, xì rung như ong (breeze, zen). Người Việt hay đọc /ð/ thành “d” hay “z”.
heard – hard C2 · /ɜː/ – /ɑː/
heard /hɜːd/ – hard /hɑːd/ · firm /fɜːm/ – farm /fɑːm/ · burn /bɜːn/ – barn /bɑːn/ · hurt /hɜːt/ – heart /hɑːt/ · stir /stɜː/ – star /stɑː/ · fur /fɜː/ – far /fɑː/ · curt /kɜːt/ – cart /kɑːt/
Mẹo: /ɜː/ miệng hơi khép, môi thả lỏng như “ơ” kéo dài (heard); /ɑː/ mở miệng rộng, lưỡi thấp như “a” kéo dài (hard).

👄 Mẹo đặt miệng (âm người Việt hay sai) (28)

Mô tả cách phát âm có đúng, dễ hiểu với người Việt không.

/θ/ Đặt đầu lưỡi giữa hai răng rồi thổi hơi (think); đừng đọc thành /t/ hay “th” tiếng Việt.
/ð/ Đầu lưỡi giữa hai răng và rung cổ (this, then); đừng đọc thành “d” hay /z/.
/ʃ/ Chu môi, lưỡi lùi hơn /s/, xì dài như “suỵt” (she); đừng đọc thành /s/.
/ʒ/ Như /ʃ/ nhưng rung cổ (vision, measure).
/tʃ/ Chu môi, bật “ch” mạnh (chair); khác /tr/ (train).
/dʒ/ Chu môi, bật như “ch” có rung (job); đừng đọc thành “gi / d” kiểu /z/.
/z/ Lưỡi sau răng, xì và rung như ong kêu (zoo, is); đừng đọc thành /s/.
/r/ Cong lưỡi lên, không chạm vòm miệng, không rung lưỡi như “r” tiếng Việt (red).
/l/ Đầu lưỡi chạm lợi trên, hơi thoát hai bên (light); cuối từ vẫn giữ lưỡi chạm lợi, đừng đọc thành “u”.
/n/ Hơi ra mũi, đầu lưỡi chạm lợi (night); đừng lẫn với /l/.
/v/ Răng trên chạm môi dưới và rung (very); đừng đọc thành “b” hay /f/.
/f/ Răng trên chạm môi dưới, thổi hơi (fine).
/p/ Đầu từ: mím môi rồi bật hơi mạnh (pen), mạnh hơn “p” tiếng Việt.
/æ/ Mở miệng rộng, giữa “a” và “e” (cat); đừng đọc thành “e”.
/ʌ/ Như “ă” ngắn, miệng thả lỏng (cup).
/ɜː/ Như “ơ” kéo dài, môi thả lỏng (bird).
/ə/ Âm “ơ” rất nhẹ, ngắn, không nhấn (about).
/ɪ/ “i” ngắn, thả lỏng (ship); khác “i” dài /iː/ (sheep).
/iː/ “i” dài, căng môi như cười (sheep).
/ʊ/ “u” ngắn, thả lỏng (book); khác /uː/ dài (food).
/uː/ “u” dài, tròn môi (food).
/w/ Tròn môi như “qu” rồi mở ra (we); đừng đọc thành /v/.
/ŋ/ “ng” ở mũi; cuối từ đừng thêm /g/ (sing).
/eɪ/ Nguyên âm đôi: lướt từ “ê” sang “i” (day).
/aɪ/ Lướt từ “a” sang “i” (my).
/əʊ/ Lướt từ “ơ” sang “u” (go).
/aʊ/ Lướt từ “a” sang “u” (now).
/ɔɪ/ Lướt từ “o” sang “i” (boy).

🧠 Câu suy luận Đọc / Nghe B1, B2 (44)

Đáp án có suy ra được từ bài không; phương án nhiễu có sai rõ không. Bấm "Bài" để xem bài.

Working from Home doc-b1-1
Bài

When Hoa's company let staff work from home two days a week, she was delighted. She would save an hour of travel every day and could finally have breakfast with her children. The first month was not as easy as she had expected. Her flat was small, so she had to work at the kitchen table. Her neighbours were building a new bathroom, and the noise made phone meetings difficult. She also noticed that she was working longer hours, because there was no clear moment when the day ended. So Hoa made some changes. She bought a small desk and put it in the corner of the bedroom. She started every home day by getting dressed as if she were going to the office, and she turned her computer off at six o'clock exactly. On Fridays she went to a café near her flat for two hours, just to see other people. Now, she says, home days are her most productive. "The secret," she explains, "is to treat home like an office, but not to let the office take over your home."

❓ What does the text suggest was Hoa's main problem at first?
✔ Work and home life were not separated. · ✗ She had too little work to do. · ✗ Her company wanted her back in the office.
A Message from the Sports Centre doc-b1-2

To say sorry for the trouble, every member will receive one extra month of membership at no cost. You do not need to do anything; it will be added to your account automatically. If you have any questions, please speak to Mai at the front desk or email info@greenpark.vn. Thank you for your patience. Green Park Sports Centre

The Boy Who Fixed Bicycles doc-b1-3
Bài

When Tuan was twelve, his bicycle broke on the way to school. His family had no money for a new one, so he took it apart in the yard and tried to repair it himself. It took him three weekends, and he made many mistakes, but in the end it worked. Soon his neighbours began bringing him their broken bikes. At first he did it for free, but his grandmother told him that his time had value. He started charging a small amount and saved every coin in a tin box. By the time he was sixteen, Tuan had saved enough to rent a tiny space at the market. He painted a sign that said 'Tuan's Bikes' and worked there every afternoon after school. His teachers were worried that he would fall behind, but his marks actually improved, because he had learned to plan his time carefully. Today Tuan is twenty-five and owns two shops. He still keeps the old tin box on his desk. "It reminds me," he says, "that every big thing starts with something small."

❓ What kind of person is Tuan, according to the story?
✔ Hard-working and patient. · ✗ Lucky and rich. · ✗ Clever but lazy.
Should Students Wear Uniforms? doc-b1-4
Bài

Last week our school asked students what they thought about uniforms. Here are two opinions. Linh, 15: "I'm in favour of uniforms. In the morning I don't have to think about what to wear, so I save time. Also, nobody can see whose family is rich and whose family is poor, so there is less pressure. Some people say uniforms are boring, but we can still show our personality with our bags, hair and shoes." Khang, 16: "I disagree. Uniforms are expensive, because we have to buy them from the school shop and they don't last long. They are also uncomfortable in hot weather. I think we should be allowed to wear our own clothes, with a few simple rules, for example no very short skirts or offensive slogans. Learning how to dress well is part of growing up." The head teacher says the school will make a decision next month after talking to parents.

❓ What is the head teacher's position at the moment?
✔ Undecided: the school wants to hear more views first. · ✗ The head teacher agrees with Linh. · ✗ The school has already stopped uniforms.
My First Job Interview doc-b1-5
Bài

I had my first job interview last Tuesday, for a part-time position at a bookshop. I was so nervous that I couldn't eat breakfast. I arrived twenty minutes early, which was a good idea, because I got lost inside the shopping centre and found the shop with only five minutes to spare. The manager, Mrs Ha, was friendly. She asked me why I wanted the job and what books I liked. I had prepared answers to these questions, so I felt more relaxed. Then she asked me something I hadn't expected: "Tell me about a time when you made a mistake." My mind went blank for a few seconds. Then I told her about a group project at school where I forgot to send an important file, and explained how I now write everything in a planner. At the end, she showed me around the shop and introduced me to two of the staff. Yesterday she phoned to offer me the job. She said that my answer about the mistake was the reason she chose me, because it showed I could learn from problems.

❓ What does the story suggest about job interviews?

Plastic in the Sea doc-b1-6
Bài

Every year, millions of tonnes of plastic end up in the oceans. Much of it comes from rivers, which carry rubbish from cities to the sea. Once it is in the water, plastic does not disappear. Sunlight and waves break it into very small pieces, called microplastics, which fish and birds eat by mistake. Scientists have found microplastics in salt, in tap water and even in the air. They are still studying what this means for human health, but many experts believe we should act now rather than wait for the results. There are some simple things everyone can do. Carrying a reusable bottle and bag is a good start. Saying no to plastic straws and choosing products with less packaging also helps. In some cities, shops give a small discount to customers who bring their own containers. Governments have a role too. Several countries have banned single-use plastic bags, and a few have introduced deposit schemes, where people get money back when they return bottles. In places where these schemes exist, far fewer bottles are found on beaches.

❓ What does the writer think about deposit schemes?
✔ They seem to work well. · ✗ They are too expensive to run. · ✗ They have not been tried anywhere yet.
The Four-Day Week doc-b2-1
Bài

When a software company in Da Nang announced that its staff would work four days a week for the same salary, many people assumed productivity would fall. Six months later, the results suggest the opposite. According to the company's figures, the amount of work completed per week has stayed roughly the same, while sick days have dropped by almost a third. Staff report that they are less tired and spend fewer hours in unnecessary meetings. "When you know you only have four days, you stop wasting time," says project manager Thu Trang. "We cancelled half our regular meetings and nobody missed them." Not everyone is convinced, however. Some customers have complained that it is harder to reach the company on Fridays, and the firm has had to introduce a rota so that a small team is always available. A few employees also admit that the longer working days can be exhausting, particularly for parents who have to collect children from school. Economists point out that the model may suit office-based businesses better than hospitals, shops or factories, where work cannot simply be done faster. Even so, the experiment has sparked a lively debate. Several other firms in the city are now running their own trials, and the company's director says she has no intention of going back. "People are not machines," she argues. "If they come back on Monday rested, they do better work. It's that simple."

❓ What does Thu Trang's comment suggest about the company before the change?
✔ Many meetings were not really necessary. · ✗ Staff often worked at weekends. · ✗ Projects were usually finished early.
Why We Forget Names doc-b2-2
Bài

Most of us have experienced it: you are introduced to someone, you shake hands, and ten seconds later their name has completely disappeared. It is embarrassing, but psychologists say it is also entirely normal. The main reason, they explain, is attention. When we meet someone new, our brains are busy with many other things at once: what the person looks like, what we are going to say, whether we are making a good impression. The name is mentioned only once, often quickly, and it simply never gets stored properly. In other words, we don't forget names; we never learn them in the first place. Names are also unusually difficult to remember because they are arbitrary. If someone tells you they are a baker, the word connects to lots of things you already know: bread, early mornings, the smell of an oven. A name like 'Hung' or 'Sarah', on the other hand, gives the brain very little to hold on to. Fortunately, there are simple techniques that help. Repeating the name immediately — "Nice to meet you, Hung" — gives your brain a second chance to record it. Linking the name to an image or to someone else you know with the same name also makes it more memorable. Finally, using the name once more at the end of the conversation can fix it in your memory. None of these tricks is perfect, but they work far better than the most common strategy, which is hoping for the best.

❓ What does the writer imply about people who say they are 'bad with names'?
✔ They usually do not pay enough attention when they first hear a name. · ✗ They have a serious memory problem. · ✗ They simply meet too many people.
Gap Year: Waste of Time or Life Lesson? doc-b2-3
Bài

After finishing school, Minh Anh surprised her parents by announcing that she would not start university immediately. Instead, she wanted to spend a year working and travelling. Her father was horrified. "He was sure I would never go back to studying," she remembers. She spent the first six months working in a hotel in Hoi An, saving most of her salary. The job was hard: long shifts, difficult guests, and a manager who rarely said thank you. But she learned to deal with complaints calmly and discovered she was good at solving problems under pressure. With her savings, she then travelled through Laos and Cambodia, volunteering at a school for two months. Teaching children with almost no resources, she says, taught her more about creativity than any classroom ever had. Critics of gap years argue that they are a luxury for the wealthy and that students lose their study habits. There is some truth in this: a gap year that consists mainly of parties on a beach is unlikely to be valuable. Universities, however, report that students who take a well-planned year out tend to be more motivated and are less likely to drop out. Minh Anh is now in her second year of a business degree. Her grades are among the best in her class, and her father has changed his mind. "He tells everyone it was his idea," she laughs.

❓ What does 'He tells everyone it was his idea' suggest?
✔ Her father is now proud of her decision. · ✗ Her father planned the gap year himself. · ✗ Her father is still angry with her.
The Return of Board Games doc-b2-4
Bài

In an age of smartphones and online games, you might expect board games to be disappearing. In fact, the opposite is happening. Sales have grown steadily for over a decade, and board game cafés, where customers pay a small fee to play from a library of hundreds of games, have opened in cities from Seoul to Ho Chi Minh City. So what explains their popularity? Part of the answer lies in a desire for real-life contact. Many young people spend their working days in front of screens, and sitting around a table with friends offers a welcome break. Unlike online games, board games require players to look at each other, negotiate and laugh together. The games themselves have also changed. Modern designers have moved away from simple luck-based games towards ones that reward planning and cooperation. In some popular titles, players work together against the game rather than against each other, which makes them attractive to people who dislike competition. The internet has, ironically, helped too. Online videos explaining the rules make it easy to learn complicated games, and crowdfunding websites allow small designers to raise money without the support of a big company. Café owner Quang believes the trend will last. "People don't come here just to play," he says. "They come to spend time with people they care about. That never goes out of fashion."

❓ Why does the writer mention Seoul and Ho Chi Minh City?
✔ To show that the trend is happening in many places. · ✗ To compare prices in the two cities. · ✗ Because board games were invented there.
Letter to the Editor: Our Disappearing Trees doc-b2-5
Bài

Dear Editor, I am writing to express my concern about the number of old trees being cut down in our city centre. In the last year alone, at least forty mature trees have been removed from Tran Phu Street to make room for wider pavements and new shops. The council claims that the trees were unsafe and that new ones will be planted. While I understand that some old trees can be dangerous in storms, I find it hard to believe that every single one was a risk. Moreover, a young tree takes twenty or thirty years to provide the same shade as the ones we have lost. The consequences are already obvious. Walking along Tran Phu Street at midday in summer is now extremely unpleasant, and several shopkeepers have told me that fewer customers stop there. Studies from other cities show that streets with trees can be several degrees cooler than those without. I would like to suggest that the council publish an independent report before removing any more trees, and that residents be consulted. I would also urge readers who share my concern to attend the public meeting at the town hall on 12 May. Yours faithfully, Nguyen Van Binh

❓ What is the writer's attitude to the council's explanation?
✔ He doubts that it is completely true. · ✗ He fully agrees with it. · ✗ He thinks every tree really was dangerous.
Sleep and Teenagers doc-b2-6
Bài

Parents often complain that teenagers stay up late and then refuse to get out of bed in the morning. It is tempting to blame laziness or phones, but scientists say biology plays a major part. During adolescence, the body releases melatonin, the hormone that makes us feel sleepy, about two hours later than it does in children or adults. As a result, many teenagers genuinely cannot fall asleep before eleven or midnight. Since they still need eight to ten hours of sleep, waking up at six for school leaves them seriously short of rest. The effects of this are not trivial. Research links lack of sleep in young people to poorer concentration, lower exam results, and even a higher risk of road accidents among new drivers. Some studies also suggest a connection with anxiety and depression. In response, a number of schools in the United States and Europe have experimented with later start times. One district that moved its school day from 7.30 to 8.30 reported better attendance and a noticeable rise in grades. Not all schools find it easy, though: later starts can cause problems with bus timetables and with parents' working hours. Phones are not completely innocent, of course. The blue light from screens can delay sleep even further. Experts recommend switching devices off an hour before bed — advice that, admittedly, many adults also ignore.

❓ Why does the writer say 'Phones are not completely innocent'?
✔ Phones make the problem worse, even though biology is the main cause. · ✗ Phones are the only reason teenagers sleep late. · ✗ Phones help teenagers fall asleep faster.

A Student's Talk about Recycling ng-b1-6
Bài

For my project, I wanted to find out how much rubbish my family produces in one week. Every day, I weighed our bin before we emptied it. The total was eleven kilos, which surprised everyone. More than half was food waste, mostly vegetable peel and rice. The rest was plastic packaging, paper and a few glass bottles. So, in the second week, we made some changes. We started putting food waste in a compost box in the garden, and my mum began taking her own bags and boxes to the market. We also stopped buying bottled water and bought a filter instead. At the end of the second week, our rubbish was down to four kilos. My dad says the compost has already made his tomatoes grow better, so now he's the biggest fan of the project.

Interview with a Mountain Guide ng-b2-1
Bài

A: Today I'm talking to Vu, who has guided climbers on Fansipan for over fifteen years. Vu, what's changed most in that time? B: Definitely the number of visitors. When I started, we'd see maybe ten people a day on the trail. Since the cable car opened, thousands go to the top every day, though most of them never actually walk. A: Has that been good for guides like you? B: It's mixed. Fewer people hire guides for the full climb, because they can get to the summit in twenty minutes. On the other hand, the ones who do choose to walk are usually serious hikers, and they're often more interested in the forest and the local villages. A: What's the biggest mistake inexperienced climbers make? B: Underestimating the weather. It can be sunny at the bottom and freezing at the top, with thick fog that comes in very quickly. People turn up in trainers and a T-shirt, and that's genuinely dangerous. A: And what keeps you doing the job after all these years? B: Honestly, it's the moment when someone who thought they couldn't make it reaches the top. You see their face change. That never gets boring.

❓ What is Vu's attitude to the cable car?
✔ He sees both good and bad sides. · ✗ He thinks it has ruined his job. · ✗ He is completely in favour of it.
A Talk on Sleep and Memory ng-b2-2
Bài

A lot of students think that staying up all night before an exam is a sensible strategy. After all, more hours of study should mean more knowledge, right? Unfortunately, the research suggests the opposite. While we sleep, particularly during the deep stages early in the night, the brain replays and strengthens what we learned during the day, moving it from short-term into long-term memory. In one experiment, students who learned a list of words and then slept remembered around twenty percent more the next day than those who stayed awake for the same period. What's more, a lack of sleep reduces our ability to concentrate and to think flexibly, which is exactly what we need in an exam. So, if you have to choose between an extra two hours of revision at midnight and two hours of sleep, the sleep is very likely to be the better investment. The best approach, of course, is to spread your revision over several weeks, so that you never face that choice in the first place.

❓ What is the speaker's attitude to studying all night before an exam?
✔ The speaker thinks it is a mistake. · ✗ The speaker recommends it. · ✗ The speaker thinks it makes no difference.

A: Fair enough. What about the ending, though? I didn't see it coming at all. B: No, the ending was clever, I'll give you that. I just wish they'd cut half an hour from the middle. A News Report on Electric Motorbikes ng-b2-4
Bài

Sales of electric motorbikes rose by more than forty percent last year, according to figures released this morning by the national transport association. The increase has been strongest in large cities, where several local governments now offer discounts of up to three million dong to residents who exchange an old petrol motorbike for an electric model. Supporters say the shift could significantly reduce air pollution, which regularly reaches dangerous levels in winter. However, the association warns that the country still lacks enough public charging stations, particularly in apartment buildings, where many residents have no private space to charge their vehicles. There are also concerns about what will happen to the millions of batteries that will need to be replaced in the coming years. A spokesperson for the Ministry of Environment said that new rules on battery recycling would be announced before the end of the year.

❓ What is the transport association's attitude?
✔ Positive about the sales, but worried about charging. · ✗ Against electric motorbikes. · ✗ Not interested in air pollution.

A Lecture on Coffee in Vietnam ng-b2-6
Bài

Most people are surprised to learn that Vietnam is the world's second-largest coffee producer, after Brazil. Coffee was introduced by French missionaries in the nineteenth century, but production only really took off in the 1990s, when economic reforms allowed farmers in the Central Highlands to plant on a large scale. Today, the vast majority of Vietnamese coffee is robusta, a variety that is easier to grow and more resistant to disease than arabica, but which has a stronger, more bitter taste. For many years it was mainly used in instant coffee and cheaper blends sold abroad. Recently, however, a new generation of growers has started focusing on quality rather than quantity, producing speciality robusta that is winning international awards. At the same time, coffee farmers face serious challenges. Rising temperatures and irregular rainfall are already reducing yields, and some experts predict that large areas of the Highlands may become unsuitable for coffee within the next few decades.

❓ Why does the speaker begin with 'Most people are surprised'?
✔ Many people do not know how much coffee Vietnam produces. · ✗ Vietnamese coffee is very expensive. · ✗ Coffee only came to Vietnam recently.
The Library That Lends Tools doc-b1-16
Bài

Most people have a drill at home that they use for about ten minutes a year. In the town of Millbrook, a group of neighbours decided this was a waste of money and space. Two years ago they opened a 'tool library' in an empty shop on the high street. It works like a normal library. Members pay a small fee of ten pounds a year and can borrow up to three tools at a time for one week. There are more than four hundred items, from ladders and saws to sewing machines and tents. Most of them were given by local people who no longer needed them. The library is run by volunteers, and every Saturday morning a retired builder called Peter teaches beginners how to use the tools safely. "Some people are nervous the first time," he says. "By the end of the morning, they're putting up shelves." Not everything has gone smoothly. In the first year, several tools came back broken, so now members must pay for repairs if something is damaged.

From Rice Field to Online Shop doc-b1-19
Bài

For thirty years, Mr Tuan sold his rice to a trader who came to his village in the Mekong Delta after every harvest. The trader decided the price, and it was always low. In 2021, Mr Tuan's daughter Linh came home from university in Can Tho with an idea. She took photos of the fields, of the family drying rice in the sun and of her father's hands holding the grain. Then she opened a small online shop and posted the photos with the story of the farm. At first, only friends and relatives bought anything. But one video of her father explaining how he grew rice without chemicals was shared thousands of times. Within a month, they had orders from Hanoi, Da Nang and even Singapore. Now the family sells about half of their rice online, at almost twice the old price. It isn't easy: Linh spends every evening answering messages and packing orders. "But for the first time," she says, "my father decides what his work is worth."

Ask the Expert: Saving for a Trip doc-b1-20
Bài

Question: I'm nineteen and I want to travel around Southeast Asia next summer, but I never have any money left at the end of the month. How can I save? – Bao Answer: Dear Bao, first of all, find out where your money goes. For two weeks, write down everything you spend, even a coffee. Most people are surprised by how much the small things add up to. Next, decide how much the trip will cost and divide that by the number of months until you leave. That's your monthly target. On the day you get paid, move that amount into a separate savings account straight away. Don't wait to see what's left at the end of the month, because there won't be anything! Finally, look for cheaper ways to have fun. Cook with friends instead of eating out, and borrow books instead of buying them. Saving shouldn't mean staying at home alone every weekend. Good luck – and send us a postcard!

Citizen Scientists doc-b2-16
Bài

Every winter, tens of thousands of ordinary people across North America spend a day counting birds. They are not professional scientists, but the information they collect has become one of the most valuable records of how bird populations are changing. This is citizen science: research in which members of the public help to gather or analyse data. The idea is not new – amateur astronomers have been reporting comets for centuries – but smartphones have transformed it. Today, volunteers photograph insects, record the calls of frogs, measure rainfall or classify images of distant galaxies from their sofas. Projects that once needed decades of fieldwork can now collect millions of observations in a single season. Critics point out that volunteers make mistakes. A beginner might confuse two similar species, or report a bird that was never there. Researchers have responded by building checks into their projects: rare sightings are reviewed by experts, and many apps ask for a photo as evidence. For the volunteers themselves, the benefits often go beyond science. Many say that taking part has changed the way they see their own neighbourhood. "I'd walked past that pond every day for years," one frog-counter explained, "and I'd never once stopped to listen."

❓ What does the frog-counter's comment suggest?
✔ The project made the volunteer notice the local area. · ✗ The volunteer found counting frogs boring. · ✗ The pond had only recently been built.
The Real Cost of Fast Fashion doc-b2-17
Bài

A T-shirt for the price of a sandwich sounds like a bargain. But according to many environmental groups, the true cost of 'fast fashion' – cheap clothes produced quickly to follow the latest trends – is paid elsewhere. Making clothes uses enormous amounts of water and energy. Cotton is a thirsty crop, and many synthetic fabrics are made from oil. Because fast-fashion items are designed to be worn only a few times, a large share of them end up in landfill or are burnt within a year or two of being bought. There are human costs too. To keep prices low, some factories pay wages that are barely enough to live on, and safety rules are not always enforced. Some brands now offer recycling schemes or 'sustainable' collections. Sceptics argue that this is largely marketing: a recycling bin in the corner of a shop does little if the same shop is releasing new designs every week. The most effective change, they say, is the simplest one – buying fewer clothes and wearing them for longer. Consumers seem to be listening, slowly. The market for second-hand clothing has grown rapidly in recent years, helped by apps that make selling an old jacket as easy as posting a photo.

❓ Why does the writer put 'sustainable' in quotation marks?
✔ To suggest the claim may not be fully true. · ✗ To show it is a technical term. · ✗ To show consumers invented the word.
Should Museums Give Objects Back? doc-b2-18
Bài

Many of the world's great museums contain objects that were taken from other countries during periods of war or colonial rule. For decades, requests to return them were usually refused. Recently, however, the debate has shifted, and a number of museums have begun to send items home. Supporters of return argue that these objects are not simply works of art but part of a living culture. A carved figure in a glass case in Europe may be a sacred object to the community it came from. Keeping it, they say, continues the injustice of the original taking. Museums that resist often make practical arguments. They point out that their collections are seen by millions of visitors from all over the world, and that the objects are carefully protected. Some also worry that if they return one item, they will face hundreds of similar claims. A middle way is appearing in some places: long-term loans, shared research projects and digital copies that allow objects to be studied anywhere. Critics on both sides are unconvinced. For some, anything less than full return is not enough; for others, even loans set a dangerous example. What seems clear is that museums can no longer ignore the question of how their collections were acquired.

❓ What can we infer about the 'middle way'?
✔ It does not fully satisfy either side. · ✗ It has ended the debate. · ✗ It is supported by most critics.
The City at Night doc-b2-19
Bài

At three in the morning, most of the city is asleep. But in a bakery near the river, Hung has already been working for two hours, shaping dough for the six o'clock opening. Across town, a cleaner is polishing the floors of an empty office tower, a nurse is checking on patients in intensive care, and a lorry driver is unloading fresh fish at the wholesale market. Night workers keep modern cities running, yet they are largely invisible. Public transport is limited after midnight, so many rely on motorbikes or expensive taxis. Shops and clinics are often closed by the time their shifts end. Working at night also has a cost to health. Research has linked long-term night work to sleep problems, weight gain and a higher risk of heart disease, partly because the body's internal clock is disturbed. Experts recommend keeping the bedroom completely dark during the day and trying to sleep at the same time even on days off. Some cities are beginning to respond. A few now have 'night mayors' – officials whose job is to represent people who live and work after dark, from improving late-night buses to making sure there are places to eat. For people like Hung, it is about time. "We feed the city," he says, "but the city forgets we exist."

❓ How does Hung feel about his situation?
✔ He feels his work is not recognised. · ✗ He enjoys working while the city is quiet. · ✗ He wants to find a day job soon.
Why Habits Are Hard to Change doc-b2-20
Bài

Think about how you brushed your teeth this morning. You probably can't remember, and that is exactly the point. Psychologists estimate that a large part of what we do each day is done out of habit, with little conscious thought. A habit forms when an action is repeated many times in the same situation. Over time, the situation itself – waking up, sitting at your desk, hearing your phone buzz – starts to trigger the action automatically. This saves the brain a great deal of effort, which is why habits are so useful. Unfortunately, the same process applies to bad habits as to good ones. This explains why simply deciding to change rarely works. Willpower is limited, and when we are tired or stressed, we fall back on whatever is automatic. Researchers suggest a more practical approach: change the situation instead of fighting the habit. If you want to stop checking your phone at work, leave it in a drawer. If you want to go running, put your shoes by the door the night before. How long does it take to build a new habit? Popular books often claim twenty-one days, but one well-known study found the average was closer to two months – and it varied enormously from person to person.

❓ Why does the writer begin by asking about brushing your teeth?
✔ To show how little we notice our habits. · ✗ To give advice about health. · ✗ To show that habits are easy to change.

There have been challenges, of course. The wind is strong up there, and we lost our first crop of beans in a storm. If your building is interested, my advice is simple: start small, and check that the roof can take the weight before you buy any soil.
