They can return the kettle to any Homeline store for a full refund or a free {3}. A receipt is not required. For more information, customers may call our helpline, {4} is open 24 hours a day.


(2) No injuries have been reported so far. · The kettle has passed every safety test without any problems. · Customers may continue to use the kettle as usual. · A receipt is required for every refund.
Câu chèn nói hậu quả của lỗi công tắc (chưa ai bị thương), trước lời dặn ngừng dùng. Qua mọi kiểm tra an toàn, cứ dùng bình thường, phải có hoá đơn đều ngược với thông báo thu hồi.
t6-office Memo · câu chèn viết lại

As you know, the marketing department will move to the fifth floor next week. Movers will arrive on Thursday at 6:00 P.M. Before you leave on Thursday, please {1} all personal items from your desk and put them in the boxes provided. {2} Computers will be moved by the IT team, so please do not pack them yourself. On Friday morning, the new offices will be ready, and you can {3} your work as usual. Thank you for your {4} during the move.


(2) Please label each box with your name and new desk number. · Please pack your computer in the same box as your personal items. · The movers arrived last Thursday and finished the job. · Boxes will not be provided, so please bring your own.
Câu chèn tiếp tục hướng dẫn đóng thùng (dán nhãn thùng). Tự đóng máy tính ngược với câu sau; thợ đã chuyển xong ngược với "tuần sau"; tự mang thùng ngược với "boxes provided".
t6-subscribe E-mail · câu chèn viết lại

Dear Mr. Brown, Your subscription to City Reader Magazine will {1} on October 31. To continue receiving the magazine without interruption, please renew before that date. {2} If you renew for two years, you will save 25 percent compared with the price of a single year. You can renew online, by phone, or by returning the enclosed form. We have enjoyed {3} you as a reader and hope you will stay with us. If you have already renewed, please {4} this message.


(2) Renewing early also gives you the best price. · Your subscription ended last month. · Two-year subscriptions are no longer available. · You do not need to renew to keep receiving the magazine.
Câu chèn dẫn tới ưu đãi giá ở câu sau ("also … the best price"). Đã hết hạn tháng trước, không còn gói 2 năm, không cần gia hạn đều ngược với thư.
t6-survey Notice · câu chèn viết lại

Pine Street Library is asking visitors to take part in a short survey about our services. The survey takes about five minutes to {1}. Your answers will help us decide how to use next year's budget. {2} Paper copies of the survey are available at the front desk, or you can answer {3} on our website. Everyone who completes the survey before May 15 will be entered into a {4} to win a tablet computer.


(2) For example, we may extend opening hours or buy more e-books. · The survey can only be completed online. · Next year's budget has already been spent. · The survey takes about an hour to complete.
"For example" cho ví dụ về cách dùng ngân sách vừa nhắc. Chỉ làm trực tuyến ngược với câu sau (có bản giấy); ngân sách đã tiêu hết, làm mất một giờ ngược với các câu trước.
t6-launch E-mail · mới

Dear customer, Thank you for attending the launch of our new kitchen collection at the Nordic Home showroom last Saturday. More than 300 guests {1} the event, and many of them tried our cooking demonstrations. {2} As promised, we are sending you a voucher for 15 percent off any item from the new collection. The voucher is valid until June 30. {3}, it cannot be combined with other offers. If you have any questions, our staff will be happy to {4} you. Kind regards, Nordic Home Customer Service


(1) attended · will attend · are attending · attend [tense]
Thì theo ngữ cảnh: buổi ra mắt diễn ra "last Saturday" (câu trước) → quá khứ: attended. Câu này đứng riêng thì không biết thì nào.
(2) We hope you enjoyed the dishes our chefs prepared. · We look forward to seeing you at the launch next Saturday. · Unfortunately, the cooking demonstrations had to be canceled. · The voucher you received at the door has now expired.
Câu chèn nối với phần trình diễn nấu ăn vừa nhắc, trước câu gửi phiếu giảm giá. Buổi ra mắt "next Saturday" sai thời gian; trình diễn bị huỷ ngược với câu trước; phiếu đã hết hạn ngược với câu sau (hạn tới 30/6).
(3) However · Therefore · For example · Similarly
Từ nối giữa câu: phiếu còn hạn tới 30/6 NHƯNG không dùng chung ưu đãi khác → However (đối lập).
(4) assist · assistance · assistant · assisted
be happy to + động từ nguyên mẫu: assist (giúp).
t6-workshop Notice · mới

The customer service workshop planned for March 14 has been moved to March 21 because the trainer is ill. The workshop {1} in Meeting Room 2 from 9:00 A.M. to 12:00 P.M. {2} Staff who registered for the original date do not need to register again. {3}, anyone who cannot attend on the new date should inform their manager by March 17. Please bring a notebook and a list of questions from your own {4} with customers. — Training Department


(1) will be held · was held · has been held · is holding [tense]
Thì theo ngữ cảnh: buổi học đã dời sang ngày 21/3 (chưa diễn ra) → tương lai bị động: will be held.
(2) Lunch will be provided after the session. · The trainer has recovered and taught the workshop yesterday. · All staff must therefore register again for March 21. · The workshop will now take place in the afternoon.
Câu chèn nói thêm chi tiết buổi học (sau giờ học có ăn trưa). Đã dạy hôm qua ngược với "dời sang 21/3"; phải đăng ký lại ngược với câu sau; học buổi chiều ngược với 9:00 – 12:00.
(3) However · For instance · As a result · Instead
Từ nối giữa câu: không cần đăng ký lại, NHƯNG ai không đến được thì báo quản lý → However.
(4) experiences · experienced · experiencing · experiment
Sau "your own" cần danh từ: experiences (trải nghiệm); experiment = thí nghiệm, sai nghĩa.
t6-stay E-mail · mới

Dear Ms. Kent, Thank you for your comments about your stay at the Bayview Hotel from August 2 to 5. We are sorry that the air conditioner in your room {1} properly during your visit. {2} Our maintenance team has since replaced the units in all rooms on the sixth floor. {3}, we would like to offer you a free night on your next stay. Simply mention this e-mail when you make your {4}. Sincerely, Paul Grant, Guest Relations Manager


(1) did not work · does not work · will not work · has not worked [tense]
Thì theo ngữ cảnh: kỳ nghỉ đã qua (2–5/8, "during your visit") → quá khứ đơn: did not work.
(2) We understand how uncomfortable this must have been in such hot weather. · We are glad that everything in your room worked perfectly. · The air conditioners on your floor were replaced just before your arrival. · Unfortunately, we are unable to offer any compensation.
Câu chèn tiếp lời xin lỗi về máy lạnh hỏng, trước câu nói đã thay máy. Mọi thứ hoạt động tốt ngược với lời xin lỗi; thay máy "trước khi chị đến" ngược với "has since replaced" (thay sau đó); không bồi thường ngược với câu tặng một đêm.
(3) In addition · Otherwise · Instead · On the other hand
Từ nối giữa câu: đã thay máy, THÊM NỮA tặng một đêm miễn phí → In addition.
(4) reservation · reserve · reserved · reserving
make your + danh từ: make a reservation (đặt phòng).
t6-supplier Memo · mới

Starting next month, our vegetables will come from Green Field Farms instead of City Produce. The farm is only fifteen kilometers away, and its trucks {1} early every morning. {2} The first delivery is scheduled for Monday, May 5. {3}, mistakes can happen with a new supplier, so please check every box against the delivery list and report any missing items to me {4}. Thank you for your help.


(1) will arrive · arrived · have arrived · were arriving [tense]
Thì theo ngữ cảnh: nhà cung cấp mới bắt đầu từ "next month" (câu trước) → tương lai: will arrive. Đứng riêng thì "arrived early every morning" cũng đúng ngữ pháp.
(2) This means our salads and soups will be made with fresher produce. · City Produce will continue to deliver our vegetables after May 5. · The farm's trucks have been delivering to us for years. · Unfortunately, the farm is too far away to deliver in the morning.
"This" = rau tới sớm mỗi sáng từ trang trại gần: câu chèn nói lợi ích. City Produce vẫn giao ngược với câu đầu; giao "nhiều năm" ngược với "nhà cung cấp mới"; quá xa ngược với "only fifteen kilometers".
(3) Of course · Instead · As a result · For example
Từ nối giữa câu: "Of course" (tất nhiên) mở ý nhắc nhở: nhà cung cấp mới có thể giao nhầm, nên kiểm từng thùng.
(4) immediately · immediate · immediacy · more immediate
Bổ nghĩa cho động từ report → trạng từ: immediately (ngay).

💼 TOEIC Đọc · Part 7 từ gần nghĩa, chèn câu [1]–[4], câu NOT, câu ý định (v139) (7)

Kiểm: từ gần nghĩa đúng nghĩa TRONG câu; câu chèn chỉ một vị trí hợp; câu NOT đúng 3 phương án có trong bài; câu ý định một cách hiểu.

t7-notice2 Notice

Riverside Public Library: New Opening Hours Beginning on Monday, May 5, the Riverside Public Library will open at 8:00 A.M. on weekdays, one hour earlier than before. [1] The change follows a survey in which many residents said they would like to visit the library before work. [2] Weekend hours will stay the same. [3] During the first week of the new schedule, staff will be at the front desk from 8:00 A.M. to help visitors set up online accounts, which can be used to reserve books and study rooms. [4] For more information, call 555-0172 or visit our Web site.


• What is the purpose of the notice? → To announce new opening hours (✗ To advertise a book sale · ✗ To request volunteers · ✗ To explain a building repair)
will open at 8:00 A.M. on weekdays, one hour earlier than before.
• What is NOT mentioned about the library? → It will add a parking area. (✗ It will open earlier on weekdays. · ✗ It asked residents for their opinions. · ✗ It offers study rooms that can be reserved.)
Câu NOT: 3 phương án kia đều có trong thông báo (giờ mở sớm hơn, cuộc khảo sát, đặt phòng học); bãi đỗ xe không được nhắc.
• In which of the positions marked [1], [2], [3], and [4] does the following sentence best belong? "Many of them also asked for more places to study quietly." → [2] (✗ [1] · ✗ [3] · ✗ [4])
"them" = residents ở câu khảo sát ngay trước; "also asked" nối với "said they would like to visit the library before work". Vị trí [4] có "study rooms" nhưng visitors không "asked" gì cả.
• In the notice, the word "follows" in the second sentence is closest in meaning to → comes after (✗ walks behind · ✗ obeys · ✗ understands)
The change follows a survey = sự thay đổi đến sau (là kết quả của) một cuộc khảo sát. follow còn nghĩa "tuân theo" (obey), "hiểu" (understand) nhưng không hợp ở đây.
t7-email3 E-mail

To: All Harlow Design staff · From: Maria Lopez, Office Manager · Subject: Kitchen renovation Dear colleagues, The second-floor kitchen will be renovated from June 9 to June 20. [1] During this time, please use the kitchen on the fourth floor, which will stay open as usual. [2] The work will include new cabinets, a larger refrigerator, and a second sink. [3] We expect the project to address the long lines at lunchtime that many of you have mentioned. [4] I apologize for any inconvenience, and thank you for your patience. Maria Lopez


• Why was the e-mail sent? → To inform staff about renovation work (✗ To invite staff to a lunch · ✗ To announce a new office manager · ✗ To ask staff for kitchen design ideas)
The second-floor kitchen will be renovated from June 9 to June 20.
• In which of the positions marked [1], [2], [3], and [4] does the following sentence best belong? "Coffee and tea will also be available in the reception area on the first floor." → [2] (✗ [1] · ✗ [3] · ✗ [4])
Câu chèn nói thêm một chỗ dùng tạm ("also available") trong lúc sửa bếp: đứng ngay sau câu bảo dùng bếp tầng 4.
• In the e-mail, the word "address" is closest in meaning to → deal with (✗ send · ✗ speak to · ✗ locate)
address the long lines = giải quyết chuyện xếp hàng dài. address còn nghĩa "ghi địa chỉ / gửi" và "nói với (khán giả)" nhưng không hợp ở đây.
• What is suggested about the second-floor kitchen? → It is often crowded at lunchtime. (✗ It will close permanently. · ✗ It was renovated last year. · ✗ It is larger than the fourth-floor kitchen.)
the long lines at lunchtime that many of you have mentioned.
t7-article3 Article

Article — Westbrook Business Journal WESTBROOK (October 2) — Greenline Bikes, a bicycle rental company founded in Westbrook four years ago, will open stations in two more cities next spring. [1] The company currently runs 60 stations across Westbrook, where riders unlock bikes with a mobile app and pay by the minute. [2] According to chief executive Hana Sato, the expansion was made possible by a new agreement with the regional train operator. [3] Under the agreement, Greenline stations will be placed outside every major train station, and train passengers will receive a 20 percent discount on rides. [4] Ms. Sato said the company also plans to add electric bikes to its fleet by the end of next year.


• What is the article mainly about? → A company's plans to expand (✗ The opening of a train station · ✗ A new type of bicycle · ✗ A change in company leadership)
will open stations in two more cities next spring.
• What is NOT mentioned about Greenline Bikes? → It sells bicycles to businesses. (✗ It was founded four years ago. · ✗ Its riders use a mobile app. · ✗ It will offer discounts to train passengers.)
Câu NOT: thành lập 4 năm trước, mở khoá bằng ứng dụng, giảm 20% cho hành khách tàu đều có trong bài; bán xe cho doanh nghiệp thì không.
• In which of the positions marked [1], [2], [3], and [4] does the following sentence best belong? "On average, each of those stations is used more than 40 times a day." → [2] (✗ [1] · ✗ [3] · ✗ [4])
"those stations" chỉ 60 trạm đang chạy ở câu ngay trước. Trạm ở thành phố mới hay ở ga tàu chưa mở nên chưa thể "được dùng 40 lần mỗi ngày".
• In the article, the word "placed" is closest in meaning to → put (✗ ranked · ✗ ordered · ✗ hired)
stations will be placed outside every major train station = trạm sẽ được đặt bên ngoài ga. place còn nghĩa "xếp hạng", "đặt hàng" (place an order), "xếp việc cho người".
t7-web Web page

Harbor View Hotel — Meeting Rooms Planning a business event in Lisbon? Harbor View Hotel offers six meeting rooms for groups of 10 to 200 people. Every room comes with free Wi-Fi, a projector, and bottled water. Catering can be arranged through our restaurant, which serves breakfast, lunch, and afternoon snacks. Guests who book a room for a full day receive free parking for up to 20 cars. To make a reservation, complete the form below at least 14 days before your event. A deposit of 30 percent is required to confirm a booking, and it is refunded in full if the booking is canceled more than seven days in advance.


• What is NOT included with every meeting room? → Free parking (✗ Wi-Fi access · ✗ A projector · ✗ Bottled water)
Wi-Fi, máy chiếu, nước có ở mọi phòng; đỗ xe miễn phí chỉ cho khách đặt phòng cả ngày.
• On the Web page, the word "arranged" is closest in meaning to → organized (✗ listed · ✗ decorated · ✗ played)
Catering can be arranged = có thể sắp xếp (tổ chức) tiệc ăn. arrange còn nghĩa "xếp (hoa) cho đẹp", "phối (nhạc)".
• What will most likely happen if a booking is canceled five days before an event? → The deposit will not be fully returned. (✗ The hotel will charge for parking. · ✗ The room will be given to another group for free. · ✗ The guest will receive a full refund.)
Chỉ hoàn đủ tiền cọc khi huỷ trước hơn 7 ngày; huỷ trước 5 ngày thì không được hoàn đủ (suy luận).
t7-letter4 Letter

Letter — Crestwood Dental Group Dear Mr. Haddad, Thank you for choosing Crestwood Dental Group. Our records show that it has been twelve months since your last check-up. Regular visits help us identify small problems before they become serious, so we recommend a check-up once a year. This year, we have extended our opening hours. Our clinic on Bay Street is now open until 7:00 P.M. on Tuesdays and Thursdays, and we see patients on Saturday mornings. To book an appointment, please call our front desk at 555-0193 or use our online booking page. Please bring your insurance card to your visit, as our patient system was recently updated. Sincerely, Anna Brooks, Patient Services


• Why was the letter sent? → To remind a patient to make an appointment (✗ To confirm a payment · ✗ To announce a new clinic location · ✗ To introduce a new dentist)
it has been twelve months since your last check-up … we recommend a check-up once a year.
• In the letter, the word "identify" in paragraph 1 is closest in meaning to → find (✗ name · ✗ copy · ✗ prove)
identify small problems = phát hiện vấn đề nhỏ. identify còn nghĩa "nhận ra, gọi tên (ai đó)" nhưng ở đây là tìm ra.
• What is suggested about Mr. Haddad's insurance information? → It may need to be entered again. (✗ It has expired. · ✗ It only covers Saturday visits. · ✗ It was sent by mail.)
bring your insurance card … as our patient system was recently updated: hệ thống mới nên có thể phải nhập lại.
t7-chat3 Text message chain

Text messages Leo Brandt (10:02 A.M.): Hi Nina, the delivery van for the Ortega order just broke down on Route 9. Nina Ruiz (10:04 A.M.): Oh no. Mr. Ortega needs those chairs for his restaurant opening at 6 tonight. Leo Brandt (10:05 A.M.): I know. The repair shop says the van won't be ready until tomorrow. Nina Ruiz (10:07 A.M.): Our second van is free this afternoon. I'll ask Sam to drive it out to you. Leo Brandt (10:08 A.M.): That would save the day. I'll wait here with the chairs. Nina Ruiz (10:09 A.M.): Great. I'll call Mr. Ortega and let him know they'll arrive by three.


• What problem does Mr. Brandt report? → A vehicle has stopped working. (✗ An order has been canceled. · ✗ Some chairs were damaged. · ✗ A road has been closed.)
the delivery van … just broke down on Route 9.
• At 10:08 A.M., what does Mr. Brandt mean when he writes, "That would save the day"? → He thinks Ms. Ruiz's plan will solve the problem. (✗ He wants to finish work early. · ✗ He plans to repair the van himself. · ✗ He is worried about the cost.)
Câu trả lời cho đề nghị dùng xe thứ hai: "save the day" = cứu nguy, giải quyết được việc gấp.
• What will Ms. Ruiz most likely do next? → Contact a customer (✗ Drive a van to Route 9 · ✗ Visit a repair shop · ✗ Cancel the delivery)
I'll call Mr. Ortega … (Sam mới là người lái xe).
t7-chat4 Online chat discussion

Online chat Grace Kim (3:15 P.M.): Has anyone booked a room for Friday's training session yet? Omar Haddad (3:16 P.M.): Not yet. Room 4B is free, but it only holds twelve people. Grace Kim (3:17 P.M.): We have eighteen people signed up. Ivy Chen (3:19 P.M.): What about the conference room on the first floor? Omar Haddad (3:20 P.M.): It's being painted this week. Ivy Chen (3:21 P.M.): Then we could split the group and run the session twice. Grace Kim (3:22 P.M.): I'm not sure the trainer can stay that long. I'll check with her and get back to you both.


• Why is Room 4B unsuitable? → It is too small. (✗ It is being painted. · ✗ It is already booked. · ✗ It has no projector.)
it only holds twelve people … We have eighteen people signed up.
• At 3:20 P.M., what does Mr. Haddad mean when he writes, "It's being painted this week"? → The conference room cannot be used. (✗ The conference room will look better soon. · ✗ He has hired a painter. · ✗ The training session should be postponed.)
Câu trả lời cho gợi ý dùng phòng hội nghị: đang sơn nên không dùng được (không phải nói chuyện màu sơn).
• What will Ms. Kim most likely do next? → Speak with the trainer (✗ Book Room 4B · ✗ Paint a room · ✗ Cancel the session)
I'll check with her (the trainer) and get back to you both.

🌏 IELTS Viết · nhãn vai trò đoạn của bài mẫu Task 2 (v140) (12)

Dùng cho 5 bài học Task 2 theo kiểu đề (ví dụ + câu làm thử "Đoạn này làm nhiệm vụ gì?"). Kiểm: nhãn đúng việc đoạn đó làm; bài mẫu đúng kiểu đề.

Đồng ý hay không t2-online
Some people believe that online learning will soon replace traditional classroom teaching. To what extent do you agree or disagree?

[Mở bài: viết lại đề + nêu quan điểm] Over the past decade, and especially since the pandemic, online courses have become a normal part of education. Some people therefore argue that traditional classrooms will soon disappear. While I accept that online learning will continue to grow, I disagree that it will replace face-to-face teaching in the near future.

[Thân bài: nhìn mặt kia (thừa nhận hoặc bác bỏ)] There are clear reasons why online learning is attractive. It is flexible, so students can study at their own pace and fit lessons around work or family commitments. It is also often cheaper, because learners do not need to travel or live near a university. For adults who want to gain a new skill, such as programming, a well-designed online course can be just as effective as a classroom.

[Thân bài: lý do ủng hộ quan điểm] However, schools and universities do much more than transfer information. In a classroom, teachers can notice immediately when a student is confused or losing interest and change their approach. Learners also develop social skills, such as working in teams and debating ideas, which are difficult to practise through a screen. In addition, many children lack a quiet place to study or a reliable internet connection at home, so a move to purely online teaching would widen the gap between rich and poor families.

[Kết luận: nhắc lại quan điểm] In conclusion, online learning is a valuable tool that will become even more common, particularly for adult learners. Nevertheless, because of the human and social benefits of classroom teaching, I believe the most likely future is a combination of the two rather than a complete replacement, with technology supporting teachers instead of taking their place.

Bàn hai quan điểm t2-cars
Some people think that city centres should be closed to private cars, while others believe that this would cause too many problems. Discuss both views and give your own opinion.

[Mở bài: viết lại đề + nêu ý mình] Traffic congestion and air pollution have led some cities to consider banning private cars from their centres. Although this policy has drawbacks, I believe that its benefits outweigh them.

[Thân bài: quan điểm thứ nhất] On the one hand, opponents argue that a ban would create serious difficulties. Shops and restaurants in the centre might lose customers who are used to driving, and some businesses could even close. People with disabilities, elderly residents and parents with young children may find it much harder to move around without a car. Critics also point out that traffic might simply move to the streets just outside the restricted area.

[Thân bài: quan điểm thứ hai] On the other hand, supporters claim that car-free centres are healthier and more pleasant places. Without cars, air quality improves and noise levels fall, so walking and cycling become safer and more attractive. Space that was used for roads and parking can be turned into parks, cafés and public squares. Several European cities that have reduced car access have found that the number of visitors to their centres actually increased.

[Ý của người viết + kết luận] In my opinion, closing city centres to private cars is a sensible policy, provided that it is introduced carefully. Good public transport, exceptions for disabled drivers and delivery vehicles, and car parks at the edge of the centre can solve most of the problems that critics mention. Cities could also start with a ban on certain days or in a small area, so that residents and businesses have time to adapt. In conclusion, while the concerns are understandable, a well-planned ban would make cities cleaner and more liveable.

Lợi và hại t2-tourism
International tourism has grown rapidly in recent decades. Do the advantages of this development outweigh the disadvantages?

[Mở bài: viết lại đề + nêu bên nào hơn] Over the last few decades, international travel has become cheaper and easier than ever, and millions of people now visit other countries every year. In my view, although this trend brings some serious problems, its advantages are greater.

[Thân bài: lợi ích] The main benefit of international tourism is economic. In many countries, tourism creates jobs not only in hotels and restaurants but also in transport, farming and handicrafts. For example, small coastal towns in Vietnam and Thailand have grown rapidly because foreign visitors spend money there. Tourism also encourages understanding between cultures. When people travel, they meet others with different traditions and ways of life, which can reduce prejudice and make them more open-minded.

[Thân bài: tác hại] However, there are clear disadvantages. Popular destinations can become overcrowded, which damages historic sites and makes life difficult for local residents. In some cities, rents have risen sharply because flats are rented to tourists rather than to local families. Air travel also produces a large amount of carbon emissions, which contributes to climate change. In addition, some traditional customs are turned into shows for visitors and lose their original meaning.

[Thân bài: cách giảm tác hại] Nevertheless, I believe these problems can be managed. Governments can limit visitor numbers at fragile sites, introduce tourist taxes to pay for maintenance, and encourage travel outside the busiest seasons. Such measures allow countries to keep the economic and cultural benefits while reducing the harm.

[Kết luận: nhắc lại bên nào hơn] In conclusion, international tourism has both positive and negative effects, but because of the jobs and cultural exchange it brings, I believe its advantages outweigh its disadvantages, provided that it is carefully controlled.

Vấn đề và giải pháp t2-obesity
In many countries, the number of children who are overweight is increasing. What are the causes of this problem, and what measures could be taken to solve it?

[Mở bài: viết lại đề + nói bài sẽ làm gì] In many parts of the world, a growing proportion of children are overweight, which increases their risk of serious illnesses later in life. This essay will examine the main causes of this trend and suggest some possible solutions.

[Thân bài: nguyên nhân, vấn đề] The first cause is diet. Processed food and sugary drinks are cheap, widely advertised and available almost everywhere, and many busy parents rely on them instead of cooking fresh meals. The second cause is a lack of physical activity. Children today spend far more time on phones, computers and televisions than previous generations, and in many cities there are few safe places for them to play outside. As a result, they consume more energy than they use.

[Thân bài: giải pháp] Several measures could help to reverse this situation. Governments could introduce taxes on sugary drinks and restrict advertisements for unhealthy food aimed at children, as some countries have already done. Schools also have an important role: they can provide healthy lunches, teach basic cooking and nutrition, and make sure that every pupil takes part in sport or exercise each day. Finally, local authorities should create more parks and safe cycling routes so that being active becomes part of children's daily lives.

[Kết luận: tóm nguyên nhân và giải pháp] In conclusion, childhood obesity is mainly caused by unhealthy diets and inactive lifestyles. It can be tackled through a combination of government regulation, better education in schools and more opportunities for outdoor activity. However, these measures will only succeed if parents also set a good example at home, since children's habits are shaped mainly by their families.

Hai câu hỏi t2-elderly
In many countries, people are living longer than ever before. What problems does this cause for society? How can these problems be reduced?

[Mở bài: viết lại đề + nói sẽ trả lời hai câu hỏi] Thanks to better healthcare and living standards, people in many countries are living much longer than previous generations. While this is a great achievement, it also creates several problems for society. This essay will discuss these problems and suggest some ways to reduce them.

[Thân bài: trả lời câu hỏi 1] The first problem is financial. As the number of retired people grows, governments must spend more on pensions, while there are fewer workers paying taxes to support them. A second problem is the pressure on health and care services. Older people are more likely to suffer from long-term illnesses, so hospitals become crowded and the cost of care rises. Finally, many elderly people experience loneliness, especially when their children have moved to other cities for work.

[Thân bài: trả lời câu hỏi 2] Several measures could help to solve these problems. Firstly, governments could gradually raise the retirement age, since people now stay healthy for longer. For example, a number of European countries have already increased the age at which people receive a state pension. Secondly, more investment in preventive healthcare, such as regular check-ups and exercise programmes, would help older people to remain independent and reduce the need for expensive treatment later. Thirdly, local communities can organise clubs, volunteer work and part-time jobs for retired people, so that they stay active and connected to others.

[Kết luận: tóm câu trả lời hai câu hỏi] In conclusion, an ageing population puts pressure on public finances, health services and family life. However, these problems can be reduced by extending working lives, focusing on prevention and helping older people to remain active members of society.

Đồng ý hay không t2-uni
Some people think that university education should be free for all students. To what extent do you agree or disagree?

[Mở bài: viết lại đề + nêu quan điểm] In many countries, students must pay high fees to attend university, and some people argue that higher education should be free for everyone. I partly agree with this view: university should be free for students who cannot afford it, but I do not believe it should be completely free for all.

[Thân bài: lý do ủng hộ quan điểm] There are strong reasons to reduce the cost of university. Education benefits society as a whole, because graduates become doctors, engineers and teachers who serve the community. If fees are too high, talented young people from poor families may decide not to study, which is a waste of their ability. Moreover, many graduates in countries with high fees start their working lives with large debts, which can delay buying a home or starting a family.

[Thân bài: nhìn mặt kia (thừa nhận hoặc bác bỏ)] On the other hand, making university free for everyone is extremely expensive. The money must come from taxes, which means that people who never went to university help to pay for the education of others, including students from wealthy families who could easily afford the fees. Free university may also encourage some students to choose courses without thinking carefully about their future, because there is no cost if they change their minds.

[Thân bài: đề xuất của người viết] In my opinion, the best solution is a system in which fees depend on family income. For instance, students from low-income families could receive free tuition and grants for living costs, while richer families pay part of the cost. Loans that are repaid only when graduates earn above a certain salary are another fair option.

[Kết luận: nhắc lại quan điểm] In conclusion, although I agree that cost should never prevent able students from going to university, I believe that free education for every student is neither fair nor affordable.

Đồng ý hay không t2-ads
Advertising aimed at children should be banned. To what extent do you agree or disagree?

[Mở bài: viết lại đề + nêu quan điểm] Children today are exposed to a huge number of advertisements on television, websites and social media. Some people think that companies should no longer be allowed to target young audiences with their marketing. I strongly agree with this view, for two main reasons.

[Thân bài: lý do ủng hộ quan điểm] Firstly, children are not able to understand adverts in the same way as adults. Young children often cannot tell the difference between a programme and an advert, and even older children may not realise that the purpose of an advert is to persuade them to buy something. Companies use bright colours, cartoon characters and free toys to attract them. For example, many fast-food restaurants offer toys with children's meals, which makes children want unhealthy food for reasons that have nothing to do with the food itself.

[Thân bài: lý do ủng hộ quan điểm] Secondly, advertising aimed at children puts pressure on parents and can harm family life. Children who see attractive products repeatedly often ask their parents to buy them, and families with little money may feel guilty when they cannot. Advertising can also make children believe that owning the latest toy, phone or brand of clothes is necessary to be happy or popular, which is a harmful message.

[Thân bài: nhìn mặt kia (thừa nhận hoặc bác bỏ)] Some people argue that a ban would hurt companies and limit freedom of speech. However, businesses could still advertise to parents, who are able to make careful decisions about what their children need. Several countries have already restricted advertising to young children without serious economic damage.

[Kết luận: nhắc lại quan điểm] In conclusion, because the young are easily influenced and marketing can encourage unhealthy habits and unnecessary spending, I believe that adverts which target them should be prohibited.

Bàn hai quan điểm t2-work
Some people prefer to work for a large company, while others prefer to work for a small one. Discuss both views and give your own opinion.

[Mở bài: viết lại đề + nêu ý mình] When choosing a job, some people prefer to work for a large company, while others would rather join a small one. Both options have clear advantages, and in my opinion the right choice depends largely on a person's stage in their career.

[Thân bài: quan điểm thứ nhất] On the one hand, large companies offer several benefits. They usually pay higher salaries and provide better insurance, pension schemes and training programmes. Employees in big organisations also have more opportunities for promotion, because there are many departments and positions. In addition, working for a well-known company looks impressive on a CV, which can help people find good jobs later.

[Thân bài: quan điểm thứ hai] On the other hand, small companies have their own attractions. Because there are fewer employees, each person usually has more responsibility and can see the direct results of their work. Staff in small firms often learn a wider range of skills, since they may be involved in sales, planning and customer service at the same time. The atmosphere is also often friendlier, and managers are easier to talk to. For example, a friend of mine who works for a small design company says that she has learned more in two years than she would have in five years at a large firm.

[Thân bài: ý của người viết] In my view, small companies are a better choice for young people at the start of their careers, because they offer valuable experience and responsibility. Later, when workers have more family commitments, the security and benefits of a large company may become more important.

[Kết luận: tóm hai bên + ý mình] In conclusion, both large and small companies have advantages, and people should choose according to their personal goals and circumstances.

Lợi và hại t2-phone
More and more people now use smartphones to manage almost every part of their lives. Do the advantages of this outweigh the disadvantages?

[Mở bài: viết lại đề + nêu bên nào hơn] Smartphones are now used for almost everything, from banking and shopping to studying and keeping in touch with friends. Although this development has some negative consequences, I believe that its advantages outweigh its disadvantages.

[Thân bài: lợi ích] The most obvious advantage is convenience. With a smartphone, people can pay bills, book tickets and check information in seconds, without visiting an office or waiting in a queue. This saves a great deal of time. Smartphones also help people stay connected. For instance, families living in different countries can make free video calls every day, which was impossible only twenty years ago. In addition, phones give access to education: learners can use language apps, online courses and digital libraries wherever they are.

[Thân bài: tác hại] However, relying on smartphones also causes problems. Many people spend hours scrolling through social media, which can reduce their concentration and affect their sleep. There are also risks to privacy and security, because phones store a great deal of personal information that criminals may try to steal. Furthermore, some people feel anxious or lost when they cannot use their phone, which suggests an unhealthy level of dependence.

[Thân bài: cách giảm tác hại] In my view, most of these disadvantages are caused by the way people use their phones rather than by the technology itself. Simple habits, such as switching off notifications, keeping phones out of the bedroom and using strong passwords, can reduce the risks considerably.

[Kết luận: nhắc lại bên nào hơn] In conclusion, smartphones make daily life faster, easier and more connected. Although they can be harmful if used carelessly, I believe that the benefits clearly outweigh the drawbacks.

Vấn đề và giải pháp t2-crime
Crime rates among young people are rising in some cities. What are the reasons for this, and what can be done about it?

[Mở bài: viết lại đề + nói bài sẽ làm gì] In some cities, young people are committing more crimes than they did in the past. This essay will explain the main reasons for this problem and suggest some measures that could help to solve it.

[Thân bài: nguyên nhân, vấn đề] There are several reasons why young people may become involved in crime. The first is a lack of opportunities. In areas with high unemployment, teenagers who leave school without qualifications may feel that they have no future, and some turn to crime to earn money. The second reason is family problems. Children who grow up without enough attention or supervision from their parents are more likely to spend time on the streets and be influenced by older criminals. A third cause is peer pressure: young people often want to be accepted by a group, even if that group breaks the law.

[Thân bài: giải pháp] To tackle this problem, action is needed in several areas. Firstly, governments should invest in education and training, so that young people have realistic chances of finding work. For example, apprenticeships that combine study with paid work can keep teenagers busy and give them useful skills. Secondly, local authorities could provide youth centres, sports clubs and music programmes, which offer safe places to spend free time. Finally, young offenders should receive support to change their behaviour, such as counselling and community service, rather than simply being sent to prison, where they may learn more about crime.

[Kết luận: tóm nguyên nhân và giải pháp] In conclusion, youth crime is mainly caused by a lack of opportunities, family problems and peer pressure. It can be reduced by better education, more activities for young people and rehabilitation instead of punishment alone.

Bàn hai quan điểm t2-env
Some people believe that individuals can do little to protect the environment and that only governments and large companies can make a difference. Others think that every individual should take responsibility. Discuss both views and give your own opinion.

[Mở bài: viết lại đề + nêu ý mình] Environmental problems such as climate change and pollution are among the most serious issues of our time. Some argue that ordinary citizens have little influence and that real change depends on the state and big business, while others insist that each of us must play a part. In my opinion, both groups have important roles to play.

[Thân bài: quan điểm thứ nhất] On the one hand, it is true that the biggest changes depend on governments and businesses. A single person recycling plastic bottles has a tiny effect compared with a government that closes coal power stations or a company that changes how it produces goods. Governments can pass laws, introduce taxes on pollution and invest in public transport and renewable energy. Large companies control supply chains that affect millions of people. For example, if a major supermarket stops using plastic packaging, the effect is far greater than the efforts of thousands of individual shoppers.

[Thân bài: quan điểm thứ hai] On the other hand, individuals should not ignore their own responsibility. When millions of people change their habits, such as using public transport, eating less meat or wasting less food, the total effect becomes significant. Moreover, individuals are also voters and customers. Their choices at elections and in shops send a clear message to politicians and businesses, encouraging them to take environmental action.

[Thân bài: ý của người viết] In my view, the two approaches support each other. Governments and companies must lead with large-scale changes, but these changes are more likely to happen when ordinary people show that they care about the environment.

[Kết luận: tóm hai bên + ý mình] In conclusion, although governments and large companies have the greatest power, individual actions still matter, both directly and by influencing those in power.

Hai câu hỏi t2-home
Nowadays, many people work from home rather than in an office. Why is this happening? Is it a positive or negative development?

[Mở bài: viết lại đề + nói sẽ trả lời hai câu hỏi] In recent years, a growing number of employees have started working from home instead of travelling to an office every day. This essay will explain why this trend is happening and argue that it is, on balance, a positive development.

[Thân bài: trả lời câu hỏi 1] There are several reasons for this change. The most important is technology. Fast internet connections, video-calling software and cloud storage mean that many office tasks can now be done from anywhere. The Covid-19 pandemic also played a major role, because it forced companies to try remote working on a large scale, and many discovered that it worked better than they expected. In addition, employers have realised that they can save money on office space if fewer staff come in each day.

[Thân bài: trả lời câu hỏi 2] I believe that this development is mainly positive. Working from home saves employees the time and cost of commuting, which can be several hours a week in large cities. This gives them more time for their families, exercise and rest. It can also reduce traffic and air pollution. For example, cities that saw many people working from home during the pandemic recorded noticeably cleaner air.

[Thân bài: mặt kia của câu trả lời 2] However, there are some disadvantages. Some people feel lonely and miss the social contact of the office, and it can be difficult to separate work from home life. Young employees may also learn less without colleagues around them. For these reasons, I think a mixed system, in which people work partly at home and partly in the office, is the best solution.

[Kết luận: tóm câu trả lời hai câu hỏi] In conclusion, working from home has become common because of new technology and the experience of the pandemic. Although it has some drawbacks, I believe it is a positive development overall.

🌏 IELTS · 8 bài tiêu chí chấm Viết / Nói (v140) (8)

Ví dụ band thấp / band cao cùng một ý và 3 câu làm thử mỗi tiêu chí. Mô tả là diễn giải của Claude, không chép band descriptors. Kiểm: hai ví dụ khác band đúng ở tiêu chí đó; đáp án làm thử đúng duy nhất.

🎯 Tiêu chí: Hoàn thành đề
Some people think that children should start school at the age of four. To what extent do you agree or disagree?
📉 Education is very important for everyone. Schools teach many subjects, and teachers help students to learn. In my country, there are many good schools, and parents want their children to study well.
📈 I disagree that children should start formal lessons at four, because at that age they learn more through play than by sitting at a desk. For example, a four-year-old who builds a tower with blocks is already learning about balance, shapes and numbers.
Đoạn thấp nói chung về giáo dục, không trả lời câu hỏi "đi học từ 4 tuổi". Đoạn cao nêu rõ quan điểm, có lý do và một ví dụ cụ thể.
• Đề: "Do the advantages of working from home outweigh the disadvantages?" Mở bài nào tốt hơn ở tiêu chí Hoàn thành đề? → Working from home has become common in recent years, and in my view its advantages, such as saving travel time, clearly outweigh its drawbacks. (✗ Working from home is a very interesting topic, and there are many different opinions about it in the modern world.)
• Đề có hai câu hỏi: "Why do many young people move to cities? Is this a positive or negative development?" Bài nào hoàn thành đề tốt hơn? → Bài có một đoạn nêu lý do (việc làm, đại học) và một đoạn nói đây là xu hướng tích cực hay tiêu cực, có lý do. (✗ Bài có ba đoạn nói rất kỹ về lý do người trẻ lên thành phố, kết luận nhắc lại các lý do đó.)
• Câu nào là ví dụ tốt hơn cho ý "Public transport reduces traffic"? → For instance, after a new metro line opened in my city, the number of cars on the main road during rush hour fell noticeably. (✗ For example, many people say that public transport is good, and it is true that it helps a lot.)
🔗 Tiêu chí: Mạch lạc
Đoạn thân bài nói về tác hại của ô tô riêng trong thành phố.
📉 Cars cause pollution. Cars are expensive. Moreover, cars are dangerous. Furthermore, cars make noise. In addition, cars need parking.
📈 Private cars create several problems in cities. The most serious is pollution, since exhaust fumes damage the health of people who live near busy roads. Cars also take up valuable space: a single parked car occupies an area that could be used for trees or wider pavements.
Đoạn thấp lặp "cars" và chồng từ nối trong khi các ý không nối với nhau. Đoạn cao có câu chủ đề, các câu sau giải thích nó, nối bằng "The most serious", "since", "also".
• Chỗ trống nên dùng từ nối nào? "Online courses are cheap and flexible. ______, many students find it hard to stay motivated without a teacher." → However (✗ Moreover · ✗ As a result)
• Câu nào nối tốt nhất sau câu "Many cities are building more cycle lanes."? → This encourages people to leave their cars at home. (✗ Cycle lanes are being built by many cities. · ✗ Moreover, cycling is a popular sport in Europe.)
• Đoạn thân bài nào mạch lạc hơn? → Đoạn mở bằng câu nêu ý chính, các câu sau giải thích và cho ví dụ cho đúng ý đó. (✗ Đoạn nêu ba ý khác nhau, mỗi ý một câu, nối bằng Firstly, Secondly, Thirdly.)
📚 Tiêu chí: Vốn từ
Đoạn nói về tác hại của việc dùng điện thoại quá nhiều.
📉 Nowadays, many people use phones a lot. This is a bad thing because phones make people have bad health and bad relationships.
📈 Nowadays, a growing number of people spend several hours a day on their smartphones. This habit can harm their physical health, for example by causing poor posture and eye strain, and it may also weaken their relationships with family and friends.
Đoạn thấp lặp "bad", "a lot", "phones". Đoạn cao dùng cụm tự nhiên (a growing number of, poor posture, eye strain, weaken their relationships) và từ chính xác hơn.
• Cụm nào đúng tiếng Anh? → heavy traffic (✗ strong traffic · ✗ big traffic)
• Câu nào có vốn từ tốt hơn? → The government should invest more in public transport to reduce congestion. (✗ The government should give more money to buses and trains to make the traffic less.)
• Đề: "Some people think university education should be free." Mở bài nào diễn đạt lại đề tốt hơn? → It is sometimes argued that students should not have to pay tuition fees to attend university. (✗ Some people think university education should be free, and I will discuss this.)
🧩 Tiêu chí: Ngữ pháp
Đoạn nói về sinh viên tốt nghiệp khó tìm việc.
📉 Many student go to university. They are study hard. But they not find job easy after graduate.
📈 Although many students go to university, a large number of graduates struggle to find a job that matches their qualifications, which suggests that a degree alone is no longer enough.
Đoạn thấp sai số nhiều (student), sai thì (are study), sai phủ định (not find). Đoạn cao có câu phức (Although…, which…) và không lỗi.
• Câu nào đúng ngữ pháp? → The number of cars on the roads has increased sharply. (✗ The number of cars on the roads have increased sharply. · ✗ The number of car on the roads has increase sharply.)
• Câu nào đa dạng ngữ pháp hơn mà vẫn đúng? → If governments taxed sugary drinks, fewer children would become overweight. (✗ Governments should tax sugary drinks. Children will be less fat.)
• Câu nào SAI ngữ pháp? → Many people believes that technology makes life easier. (✗ Many people believe that technology makes life easier. · ✗ It is widely believed that technology makes life easier.)
🌊 Tiêu chí: Trôi chảy và mạch lạc
Do you like cooking?
📉 Yes. Cooking. I like… um… cooking. Yes.
📈 Yes, quite a lot, actually. I usually cook dinner at weekends because it helps me relax after a busy week, and my favourite thing to make is a simple chicken curry.
Câu thấp ngắn, lặp, ngập ngừng. Câu cao trả lời rồi phát triển (because, and), nghe tự nhiên.
• Câu hỏi Part 3: "Why do people like shopping online?" Câu trả lời nào trôi chảy và mạch lạc hơn? → Mainly because it's convenient. You can compare prices in a few minutes and have things delivered to your door, so it saves a lot of time. That said, some people still prefer shops because they want to see the product first. (✗ Online. Shopping online. Because… it is… easy. Yes, easy. · ✗ Shopping online is shopping that people do on the internet.)
• Đang nói thì quên một từ. Nên làm gì? → Nói vòng bằng từ khác ("the thing you use to…") rồi nói tiếp. (✗ Dừng hẳn, im lặng tới khi nhớ ra từ. · ✗ Nói từ đó bằng tiếng Việt.)
• Câu nào là cách mở đầu tự nhiên khi cần vài giây để nghĩ? → That's an interesting question. I'd say… (✗ Um… um… um… · ✗ I don't know.)
📚 Tiêu chí: Vốn từ khi nói
Describe your hometown.
📉 My hometown is good. It has good food and good people. It is very good.
📈 I'm from Hue, a fairly small city in central Vietnam. It's famous for its historic buildings and its spicy noodle soup, and the pace of life is much slower than in Hanoi, which I really appreciate.
Câu thấp lặp "good". Câu cao dùng từ cụ thể (historic buildings, spicy noodle soup, the pace of life, appreciate).
• Câu nào nghe tự nhiên khi nói về sở thích? → I'm really into photography at the moment. (✗ I very like photography at the moment. · ✗ Photography is my hobby which I like it.)
• "How do you usually travel to work?" Câu nào có vốn từ tốt hơn? → I usually commute by motorbike. It takes about twenty minutes, although the rush-hour traffic can be a nightmare. (✗ I go by motorbike. It is fast. Sometimes it is slow because many cars.)
• Quên từ "umbrella". Nên nói thế nào? → "…the thing you open over your head when it rains." (✗ Dừng lại và nói "Sorry, I don't know the word." · ✗ "…the rain hat… no… the… um…")
🧩 Tiêu chí: Ngữ pháp khi nói
Describe a trip you enjoyed.
📉 Last year I go to Da Lat with my friend. We visit many place. It is very beautiful and we are happy.
📈 Last year I went to Da Lat with two friends. We'd been planning the trip for months, so when we finally arrived, we were really excited. If I had more time, I'd definitely go back.
Câu thấp kể quá khứ bằng hiện tại (go, visit), thiếu -s (many place). Câu cao dùng quá khứ đơn, quá khứ hoàn thành tiếp diễn, câu điều kiện, đúng hết.
• "Have you ever been abroad?" Câu nào đúng? → Yes, I've been to Thailand twice. (✗ Yes, I have go to Thailand two times. · ✗ Yes, I go to Thailand last year two times.)
• "What will cities be like in the future?" Câu nào có ngữ pháp đa dạng hơn? → I think cities will become greener, because if people use electric cars, the air will be much cleaner. (✗ Cities are green. People use electric cars. Air is clean.)
• Lỡ nói "Yesterday I go to the market…". Nên làm gì? → Sửa nhanh "…I went to the market" rồi nói tiếp. (✗ Dừng lại xin lỗi giám khảo rồi kể lại từ đầu. · ✗ Không sửa, nói tiếp bằng thì hiện tại cho đều.)
👄 Tiêu chí: Phát âm
Đọc to: "I watched three films last weekend."
📉 I watch tree film las weeken.
📈 I watched three films last weekend.
Cách đọc thấp bỏ âm cuối (/t/ của watched, /z/ của films, /t/ của last, /d/ của weekend) và đọc three /θriː/ thành tree /triː/ (cái cây): người nghe mất nghĩa quá khứ, số nhiều. Cách đọc cao nói rõ các âm đó.
• Từ "photography" nhấn trọng âm ở đâu? → pho-TO-gra-phy (✗ PHO-to-gra-phy · ✗ pho-to-GRA-phy)
• Đuôi -ed của "started" đọc thế nào? → /ɪd/ (thêm một âm tiết: star-tid) (✗ /t/ · ✗ /d/)
• Câu hỏi "Do you like music?" cuối câu nên lên hay xuống giọng? → Lên giọng (câu hỏi Yes / No) (✗ Xuống giọng như câu kể · ✗ Giữ đều, không lên không xuống)

🌏 IELTS Nói · nhóm thẻ Part 2, kiểu câu hỏi Part 3 (v140) (11)

Máy xếp theo câu chữ của thẻ / câu hỏi (ielts.js IE_P2CAT, IE_P3TY) cho 2 bài học nhận nhóm / kiểu. Kiểm: xếp đúng nhóm; khung trả lời mỗi nhóm đúng và dùng được.

👤 Người
Ai, quen thế nào, người đó ra sao (tính cách, việc làm), vì sao ảnh hưởng tới bạn; kể một chuyện cụ thể.
Thẻ: Describe a person who has influenced you.
📍 Nơi chốn
Ở đâu, đến khi nào và với ai, ở đó có gì, làm gì; cảm giác và vì sao thích (muốn quay lại).
Thẻ: Describe a place you visited that you would like to go back to. · Describe a city you would like to live in.
🎁 Đồ vật
Là gì, có từ khi nào hoặc ai cho, dùng thế nào, vì sao quan trọng với bạn.
Thẻ: Describe a piece of technology you use every day. · Describe an object that is important to you. · Describe something you saved money to buy.
🎉 Sự kiện, trải nghiệm
Kể theo thời gian: khi nào, ở đâu, chuyện gì xảy ra, kết thúc ra sao; dùng thì quá khứ; nói cảm xúc lúc đó.
Thẻ: Describe an important change in your life. · Describe a time when you helped someone. · Describe a celebration or festival you enjoyed. · Describe a difficult decision you had to make. · Describe a time when someone gave you good advice. · Describe a lesson or class you really enjoyed. · Describe a time when you were in a noisy place. · Describe a tradition in your country that you like.
🎯 Hoạt động, kỹ năng, mục tiêu
Là gì, bắt đầu hoặc học thế nào, khó ở đâu, có lợi gì; mục tiêu thì nói kế hoạch (going to, would like to).
Thẻ: Describe a skill you learned that you think is useful. · Describe a goal you would like to achieve in the future.
📖 Sách, phim, mạng
Tên, nội dung chính trong 2–3 câu (đừng kể hết), biết qua đâu, điều làm bạn nhớ hoặc thấy hữu ích.
Thẻ: Describe a book or film that made you think. · Describe a website or app you find useful.
💭 Ý kiến, nên hay không
Nêu ý kiến rõ (I think… / I'd say…) → lý do → ví dụ → có thể nhìn mặt kia (That said…).
Câu hỏi: What qualities make someone a good role model? · Should famous people be expected to behave well in public? · Which skills are most important for young people today? · Should films be used to teach history? · Should schools teach children to help in the community? · Should children be allowed to use smartphones? · Is it important to set goals in life? · Should success be measured by money? · Who should young people ask for advice when making decisions? · Do people make better decisions when they are older? · Should children be allowed to make important decisions for themselves? · Do people today own too many things? · Who do young people usually ask for advice? · Do people spend too much time on their phones? · What makes a good teacher? · Should there be laws to control noise? · Should children be taught how to manage money? · Should old traditions change to fit modern life?
⚖️ So sánh hai lựa chọn, hai nhóm
Nói rõ bên nào hơn hoặc "it depends on…"; so từng điểm (cheaper, more flexible…), mỗi bên một ví dụ.
Câu hỏi: Is it better to learn skills at school or at work? · Is it better to change jobs often or stay in one job? · Are people less helpful in big cities than in small towns? · Has technology made people's lives easier or more stressful? · How do celebrations differ between generations? · Is it better to get advice from friends or from experts? · Is it better to learn in a group or alone? · Is it better to save money or to enjoy it now?
⏳ Xưa và nay
Dùng đúng thì: In the past, people used to…; Nowadays…; Over the last few years, … has changed; nói lý do của thay đổi.
Câu hỏi: Do young people today have different role models from in the past? · Do people read less than they used to? · How has technology changed the way we enjoy stories? · How have cities in your country changed in recent years? · Do young people today have more opportunities than their parents had? · Are festivals becoming too commercial? · How has advertising changed what people buy? · How has technology changed the way students learn? · Are people today more used to noise than in the past? · Are young people losing interest in traditions?
🔮 Dự đoán tương lai
Dự đoán có mức độ: will probably, might, I doubt that…; dựa vào xu hướng hiện nay để giải thích.
Câu hỏi: Will people travel more or less in the future? · Will machines replace people in skilled jobs? · What technology do you think will be important in the future? · Will online learning replace schools?
🔍 Nguyên nhân, ảnh hưởng, giải pháp
Nêu 1–2 nguyên nhân hoặc ảnh hưởng chính, giải thích từng cái; câu "How can…?" thì đưa giải pháp cụ thể: ai làm, làm gì.
Câu hỏi: Why do people like to travel to new places? · What are the effects of tourism on local communities? · Why do some people find change difficult? · Why do some people volunteer to help others? · Why are traditional festivals important? · Why do people keep things they no longer use? · Why do some people not like receiving advice? · What are the advantages of living in a big city? · Why do many young people move to cities? · How can cities be made better places to live? · What are the dangers of sharing information online? · What are the main sources of noise in cities? · Why do some people find it hard to save? · Why are traditions important for a country?

🌏 IELTS Nghe · phần mặt bằng tòa nhà (v140) (1)

Part 2 một người nói: 2 câu chọn HAI + 3 trắc nghiệm, 5 câu mặt bằng (chữ A–H, 3 chữ thừa). Phía trên mặt bằng = hướng bắc; lối vào ở dưới. Kiểm: lời chỉ đường dẫn tới đúng chữ; đáp án duy nhất.

Volunteering at a community centre
Lời

Good morning, everyone, and thank you for coming to this welcome session for new volunteers. My name's Sarah Okafor, and I'm the manager here at Westbury Community Centre. As most of you know, the centre has just reopened after eight months of building work. The old building was more than fifty years old, and the main problem was the heating, which broke down almost every winter. So the biggest change is one you won't actually see: a completely new heating system. We've also put in a lift, so every part of the building can now be reached by wheelchair users. Our opening hours have changed slightly. We used to stay open until nine in the evening, but so few people came in late that we now close at eight o'clock from Monday to Friday. At weekends we're open from ten until four, the same as before. Now, a word about what we'd like you to do. You won't need to deal with room bookings or payments, because the office staff look after all of that. Your main job is to welcome visitors and help them find the activity or room they've come for. On busy days, we may also ask you to help set out chairs in the main hall. Parking is a bit of a problem here. The small car park behind the centre is only for visitors with disabilities and for deliveries, so please use the public car park on Mill Street. It's free after six, but better still, come by bus or bike if you can. And if the fire alarm ever goes off, everyone should leave by the nearest door and meet on the grass area across the road. Please don't wait in the car park, because the fire engines need that space. Right, let's look at the plan of the ground floor. We're in the entrance area at the bottom of the plan, and reception is straight in front of you. Behind reception, a corridor runs all the way up to the main hall at the top. As you walk along the corridor, the first room on your left is the café. It's run by a local charity, and volunteers get a ten per cent discount. People often expect the first aid room to be next to reception, but in fact it's directly opposite the café, on the right-hand side of the corridor. Carry on along the corridor, and the next room on the left is the computer room, where we run free classes for older residents. The art studio can only be reached through the main hall. Go into the hall and you'll see a door on the right-hand side. The studio is through there, in the top right-hand corner of the building. And finally, if you're helping families, the children's play room is easy to find. As you come in through the entrance, turn right straight away, before you get to reception. It's in the bottom right-hand corner. OK, any questions so far?

Mặt bằng (x, y, rộng, cao trên lưới 10 × 10): Main hall (2, 0, 6, 3) · Corridor (4, 3, 2, 4) · Reception (4, 7, 2, 2) · Entrance (4, 9, 2, 1) · A (0, 0, 2, 3) · B (8, 0, 2, 3) · C (0, 3, 4, 2) · D (6, 3, 4, 2) · E (0, 5, 4, 2) · F (6, 5, 4, 2) · G (0, 8, 3, 2) · H (7, 8, 3, 2)
• Which TWO changes have been made to the building? → a new heating system + a lift (✗ a bigger car park · ✗ solar panels on the roof · ✗ longer opening hours)
• From Monday to Friday, the centre now closes at → 8 pm. (✗ 9 pm. · ✗ 4 pm. · ✗ 6 pm.)
• The main job of the volunteers is to → help visitors find where to go. (✗ take room bookings. · ✗ collect payments. · ✗ set out chairs in the hall.)
• Volunteers who drive should park → on Mill Street. (✗ behind the centre. · ✗ outside the main entrance. · ✗ at the bus station.)
• Café → E (Đi dọc hành lang từ quầy lễ tân: phòng đầu tiên bên trái.)
• First aid room → F (Đối diện quán cà phê, bên phải hành lang; bẫy: không ở cạnh quầy lễ tân.)
• Computer room → C (Đi tiếp hành lang: phòng kế tiếp bên trái.)
• Art studio → B (Chỉ vào được qua hội trường: cửa bên phải hội trường, góc trên bên phải.)
• Children's play room → H (Vào cửa là rẽ phải ngay, trước quầy lễ tân: góc dưới bên phải.)

🌏 IELTS Đọc · 2 bài dài 750–900 từ (v140) (2)

Bài học thuật dài như đề thật; người / nghiên cứu trong bài là hư cấu, sự kiện thật chỉ nêu chung. Kiểm: đáp án đúng và duy nhất; NOT GIVEN không suy ra được; tiêu đề thừa không khớp đoạn nào.

The Hidden Rules of Waiting in Line C1 · 792 từ
Bài

A. Most of us spend a surprising amount of our lives waiting: at supermarket checkouts, at bus stops, on the telephone to a bank. Yet research suggests that the number of minutes we wait is only part of the story. What matters just as much is how the wait feels. Two people who stand in the same line for the same length of time may leave with completely different impressions, one irritated and the other hardly aware that any time has passed. For this reason, psychologists who study waiting distinguish between actual time, which can be measured with a clock, and perceived time, which exists only in the mind of the person waiting. B. One of the most consistent findings is that empty time feels longer than occupied time. This explains why hotels and offices often place mirrors beside lifts: people who are checking their appearance complain less about slow lifts than people with nothing to look at. A well-known story from an airport in the United States makes the same point. Passengers complained that they had to wait too long for their luggage, even though the waiting time was close to the industry average. Instead of employing more baggage handlers, the airport moved arriving flights to gates further from the baggage hall, so that passengers spent longer walking and less time standing still. Complaints fell sharply, although the total time from plane to suitcase had not changed. C. Uncertainty also stretches time. A wait of unknown length is harder to bear than a longer wait that people can predict, because they cannot plan their time or relax. Many organisations therefore announce expected waiting times, and some go further. Some theme parks are said to display waiting times for rides that are slightly longer than the real ones, so that visitors who reach the front early feel they have gained something. Telephone services that tell callers their position in the queue rely on the same idea: knowing that you are fourth in line is more comforting than hearing the same recorded music again and again. D. Even more important than length, however, is fairness. According to Dr Helena Marsh, a psychologist who has filmed queues in shops and stations for more than a decade, people will accept long waits calmly as long as they believe everyone is being treated equally. What makes them angry is seeing someone push in, or watching a person who arrived later being served first. This is one reason why many banks and post offices now use a single line that feeds several counters, sometimes called a serpentine queue. With separate lines, customers constantly compare their progress with the line next to them, and almost everyone believes that the other line is moving faster. E. The mathematics behind these systems is older than many people realise. In the early twentieth century, a Danish engineer named Agner Erlang, who worked for a telephone company in Copenhagen, wanted to know how many lines an exchange needed so that callers would rarely find every line busy. His calculations became the basis of queuing theory, which is now used to plan everything from hospital emergency departments to supermarket checkouts. One of its clearest lessons concerns the single queue. When one line serves several counters, no customer can be trapped behind a slow transaction while others move ahead, so the chance that one unlucky person waits far longer than everyone else falls sharply. F. The unwritten rules of queuing are not the same everywhere. In some countries, an orderly line forms almost automatically, and leaving a gap or standing too close to the person in front can cause offence. In others, a crowd gathers at a counter without any visible order, yet Dr Marsh found that people in such crowds usually knew exactly who had arrived before them and expected to be served in that sequence. 'What looks like chaos to a visitor often has a clear structure,' she says. Her recordings also showed that people were more tolerant when a person ahead of them was joined by a single companion than when several friends arrived at once. G. Technology is now changing the experience again. Restaurants and clinics increasingly use virtual queues, in which customers register their place and receive a text message when it is their turn, leaving them free to go for a walk or do some shopping in the meantime. For many people this removes the most frustrating part of waiting, since the time is no longer empty. Yet virtual queues are not without problems. They can disadvantage people who do not own a smartphone, and they remove the shared experience of standing together, which some researchers believe helps strangers to accept delays more calmly. Waiting, it seems, will never be simply a matter of minutes.


• Tiêu đề đoạn A → Why minutes are not the whole story
• Tiêu đề đoạn B → Keeping people busy while they wait
• Tiêu đề đoạn C → The cost of not knowing
• Tiêu đề đoạn D → Something people care about more than length
• Tiêu đề đoạn E → Early calculations from the telephone industry
• Tiêu đề đoạn F → Unwritten rules that vary from place to place
• Tiêu đề đoạn G → Waiting without standing in line
Tiêu đề thừa: The financial cost of queues to businesses · How children learn to take turns · Why some shops prefer long queues
• The airport reduced complaints by making the total wait for luggage shorter. → FALSE (Đoạn B: khiếu nại giảm "although the total time from plane to suitcase had not changed".)
• Some theme parks show waiting times that are longer than the real ones. → TRUE (Đoạn C: display waiting times for rides that are slightly longer than the real ones.)
• Erlang's calculations were originally made to help plan supermarket checkouts. → FALSE (Đoạn E: Erlang làm cho công ty điện thoại, muốn biết tổng đài cần bao nhiêu đường dây; quầy siêu thị là chỗ dùng lý thuyết này về sau.)
• Virtual queues are now more common than traditional lines in restaurants. → NOT GIVEN (Đoạn G chỉ nói nhà hàng "increasingly use" hàng chờ ảo, không so cái nào phổ biến hơn.)
• Hotels and offices often place ______ beside lifts so that people have something to look at. → mirrors (Đoạn B: hotels and offices often place mirrors beside lifts.)
• A single line that feeds several counters is sometimes called a ______ queue. → serpentine (Đoạn D: sometimes called a serpentine queue.)
• Virtual queues can disadvantage people who do not own a ______. → smartphone (Đoạn G: people who do not own a smartphone.)
Building with Fungi C1 · 762 từ
Bài

A. The construction industry has a serious environmental problem. Concrete is the most widely used building material in the world, and producing the cement that holds it together is responsible for around eight per cent of global carbon dioxide emissions. Steel and fired bricks also require enormous amounts of energy to make. As governments set targets for reducing emissions, architects and engineers have begun to look for materials that can be grown rather than manufactured. One of the most unusual candidates is mycelium, the hidden part of a fungus that lives underground or inside rotting wood, and which most people never notice. B. Mycelium consists of a dense network of thin threads called hyphae, which spread through soil and dead plant matter in search of food. As they grow, the threads bind loose material together into a solid mass. Producers take advantage of this by mixing fungal spores with agricultural waste such as sawdust, hemp or corn husks and packing the mixture into a mould. Over five to ten days, the fungus digests part of the waste and fills the mould with a tight web of threads. The block is then removed and dried in an oven, which kills the fungus and stops it growing any further. The result is a light, firm material that can be shaped in almost any way, from thin panels to large bricks. C. The idea is less experimental than it sounds. For more than a decade, mycelium has been sold commercially as packaging, where it replaces polystyrene foam in boxes used to protect computers, furniture and wine bottles during transport. Unlike polystyrene, which can remain in the environment for centuries, the fungal packaging breaks down in a garden compost heap within a few weeks. Architects have also experimented on a larger scale. In 2014, a tower built from mycelium bricks stood for a summer in the courtyard of an art museum in New York. It was designed to be temporary, and when the exhibition ended, the bricks were broken up and composted, leaving almost no waste. D. Laboratory tests have produced encouraging results. Dr Leila Navarro, a materials scientist who has studied fungal composites for several years, found that panels made from mycelium kept heat in about as well as some of the foam boards commonly used to insulate walls. They also absorbed sound effectively, which could make them useful in offices and schools. Perhaps most surprisingly, the panels did not catch fire easily. 'When a flame touches the surface, it forms a layer of char that protects the material underneath,' Navarro explains. Many plastic foams, by contrast, burn quickly and release toxic smoke. E. However, the material has clear weaknesses. Dr Marcus Feld, a structural engineer, points out that mycelium is far weaker than concrete or timber when it is squeezed, so it cannot be used for walls or columns that hold up a building. It also absorbs water, and if it stays damp, other organisms can grow on it and cause it to decay. 'Used in the right place, it performs very well,' Feld says, 'but that place is inside a dry building, in panels and linings that carry no weight. Anyone who promises mycelium skyscrapers is ignoring the engineering.' F. There are also practical obstacles to wider use. Because each batch is grown rather than manufactured, its properties can vary depending on the waste used, the temperature and the humidity, and builders need materials that behave predictably. Producing large quantities requires space and careful control of conditions, which adds to the cost. Some producers have responded by growing panels in sealed rooms where conditions never change. According to Dr Anna Kowal, who studies the regulation of the construction industry, a further problem is that building codes were written for conventional materials. 'A new material may perform perfectly well,' she says, 'but if the rules have no category for it, getting permission to use it is slow and expensive.' Until codes are updated, she expects mycelium to be used mainly in small projects. G. Supporters argue that the greatest advantage of mycelium lies in what happens at the end of a building's life. Concrete and plastic foam usually end up in landfill, whereas fungal panels can be returned to the soil, completing a cycle that began with agricultural waste. Few experts believe that mycelium will replace concrete. A more realistic future, they suggest, is one in which grown materials are combined with timber and other low-carbon products, each used where its strengths matter most. If that happens, buildings may become not only cleaner to construct but also easier to take apart.


• a description of the stages in producing the material → đoạn B (Đoạn B: trộn bào tử với phế phẩm, cho vào khuôn, 5–10 ngày, sấy trong lò.)
• an example of a structure that was intended to last only a short time → đoạn C (Đoạn C: toà tháp ở New York "was designed to be temporary".)
• a reason why the material should not support the weight of a building → đoạn E (Đoạn E: yếu hơn bê tông, gỗ khi bị nén nên không dùng cho tường, cột chịu lực.)
• a reference to what happens to the material when a building is no longer needed → đoạn G (Đoạn G: hết đời toà nhà, tấm nấm trả về đất thay vì vào bãi rác.)
• How well the material performs depends on where in a building it is used. → Dr Marcus Feld (Đoạn E: Feld: "Used in the right place, it performs very well".)
• A protective layer forms on the material when it meets a flame. → Dr Leila Navarro (Đoạn D: Navarro: "it forms a layer of char that protects the material underneath".)
• Official rules are slowing down the use of the material. → Dr Anna Kowal (Đoạn F: Kowal: building codes were written for conventional materials.)
• The material reduces noise as well as keeping heat in. → Dr Leila Navarro (Đoạn D: Navarro: giữ nhiệt như tấm xốp và "absorbed sound effectively".)
• What does the writer say about mycelium packaging? → It breaks down much faster than polystyrene. (✗ It is cheaper to produce than polystyrene. · ✗ It has only recently been sold in shops. · ✗ It is mainly used to protect food.)
• What is the main purpose of paragraph F? → to explain why the material is not yet widely used (✗ to compare the costs of mycelium and concrete · ✗ to describe a successful commercial project · ✗ to argue that building codes should be abolished)
• What is the writer's overall view of mycelium as a building material? → It will probably be used alongside other materials rather than replace concrete. (✗ It will soon replace concrete in most new buildings. · ✗ It is too unreliable to be worth further research. · ✗ It is only suitable for packaging.)
• What are the thin threads that make up mycelium called? → hyphae (Đoạn B: thin threads called hyphae.)
• Where is the block dried to stop the fungus growing? → in an oven / an oven / oven (Đoạn B: dried in an oven, which kills the fungus.)
• In which city did a mycelium tower stand for a summer? → New York (Đoạn C: in the courtyard of an art museum in New York.)
